## Problem / Why

The biome-driven pipeline shipped (build 2026-07-12.4, TGV 33) with six recorded deferrals plus one owner observation. Each is now investigated with measurements:

| # | Item | Investigated finding (evidence in §WS sections) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Terracing on steep slopes | **Intrinsic voxel quantization, NOT caused by the flip** (flag A/B identical: wideTerrace 0.238/0.237, 0.222/0.219, 0.219/0.224). ~22-24% of macro-steep mountain cells are flat treads. Tread AREA is conserved under added smooth signal — only 2 levers exist |
| 2 | BIOME_STYLE all-zero (biomes lack terrain *character*) | Cost mechanism isolated: per-column `classifyBiome` inside `terrainSurface` recomputes T/H (2 fresh 3-octave fbms) AND redundantly recomputes C/E → measured ~2.28× on non-cache callers. Amortization options identified |
| 3 | forceSingleBiome forces label, not shape (Q6) | Single-site fix confirmed: `terrainSurface`'s relief-sourcing line; `classifyBiome` already short-circuits |
| 4 | Larger climate regions (owner intent) | Autocorr ≈ ⅓·(1/freq); doubling = halving 5 frequency literals (2 of which are bare constants NOT behind `paramFreq` — C is untouched by the Biome Size slider today) |
| 5 | Future biomes (desert/tundra/snowy_peaks/ocean) | Full touchpoint list (17 sites). Desert needs a cascade OR-branch + M10 exemption; tundra CANNOT reuse the snow ladder (the −13 `bandShift` floor is CCR-TERRAIN-010's fix — needs a separate low-elevation snow path); **ocean CANNOT key off C: measured Pearson r ≈ −0.012** (≈ zero) — needs `getOceanFactor` as its own axis |
| 6 | Hydrological rivers | **Prototyped and proven feasible**: region-1024/step-32 lattice + pit-centered flood-and-spill → 100% ocean connectivity ×3 seeds, query ≤1.3× ribbon cost, build amortizes <0.1% of chunk gen, seam-free by construction |
| 7 | Legacy excision + flag removal | Full blast radius enumerated (15 `mountain_foothills` sites, 26 `useNewTerrain`, 7 browser suites delete, 1 edits). Owner-gated |

## Approach

Ship the seven workstreams as independent flag-gated or additive efforts in dependency order, batching `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` bumps into at most TWO player-visible regens (§Version impact). Every workstream keeps the established discipline: prototype-first in Node with the real extracted functions, metric gates an agent can run autonomously (new metrics M15–M20 DEFINED in `tools/lib/biome-metrics.mjs` — which currently tops out at M14 — and surfaced/gated via `tools/biome-pipeline-checks.mjs`), flag-OFF byte-identity proven by the gold-standard direct-diff method (2401+ cols × 3 seeds vs a pre-edit snapshot), browser suite green, in-game eyeball last.

**Rejected alternatives:**
- *One mega-implementation, single flip* — couples seven risk profiles; a hydrology bug would block a style-tuning ship. Rejected.
- *Seven separate CCRs* — the items share harness work, version-bump batching, and sequencing constraints; splitting hides the dependencies this document exists to encode. Rejected (workstreams may still be EXECUTED by separate hand-offs; WS6 in particular is big enough that its implementer should treat its section as a self-contained CCR).
- *Terracing: add high-frequency detail / dither / reduce amplitude* — all three MEASURED as non-fixes (detail ±0.005 no change; dither slightly worse; amplitude reduction BACKFIRES 0.22→0.33-0.41). Enter these in the do-not-retry ledger at WS1 completion. Rejected with evidence.

## Version impact

- `VOXEX_BUILD`: bump per shipped phase + `VOXEX_RECENT_CHANGES` entries (always)
- `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION`: **two planned bumps, batched**:
  - **Bump A (33→34) — "terrain character batch"**: WS2 styles flip + WS4 region-size flip + WS5 new biomes flip + WS1 terracing warp (if adopted after eyeball) + WS3 shape-forcing. All are staged behind additive tunables whose defaults are byte-identical to today (WS1 `TERRACE_WARP_*`=0, WS2 styles=0, WS4 `FREQ_*`=current) OR are output-changes gated by an already-set condition (WS3 = only `forceSingleBiome` worlds), so the additive PLUMBING can land anytime; the CHANGED VALUES flip TOGETHER in one regen. **EXCEPTION — WS5:** new centroid/style rows + material branches have NO byte-identical staging (a new row changes `classifyBiome` output the moment it lands), so they must land WITHIN the Bump-A commit series itself, not as pre-staged additive plumbing (see WS5's own note). There is no NEW flag hiding any of these value changes — `biomeDrivenTerrain` is already the default-ON path.
  - **Bump B (34→35) — hydrological rivers** (WS6 flip). Kept separate: different risk class, big system.
  - WS7 (excision) removes only flag-OFF-reachable code → **no bump** (flag-ON output unchanged; prove by direct diff).
- `CURRENT_CACHE_VERSION` / `SETTINGS_VERSION`: no (re-evaluate per-WS if material/light adjacency changes surface unexpectedly)

# WS1 — Terracing (owner follow-up #7)

**Measured root cause** (harness: `tools/scratch/terrace-scan2.mjs`, `viz-and-struct.mjs`, `final-fixes.mjs`, `decompose.mjs`, `spline-audit.mjs`; evidence PNG `terrace-flank-1337.png`): flooring a smooth float surface whose ridged-multifractal naturally alternates steep faces with near-flat benches. Attribution on steep fall-lines: mountain cores ~87% fractal-morphology quantization (relief PINNED at 0.93 for 15.3% of mountain cols — splines irrelevant there); foothills ~52% relief-spline lift (smoothstep knots have exactly zero derivative — `spline()` uses `t·t·(3−2t)`). Flag A/B: **identical** — the biome-driven flip did not cause or worsen it. Baseline metric: wideTerrace-frac (treads/macro-steep cells) = 0.238/0.222/0.219 (seeds 1337/42/9001); guard metric plainsRough = 0.019.

**The two real levers (measured):**
1. **Relief-gated contour-break domain warp** — `H(x,z) = terrainSurface(x+w, z+w2)`, w = relief-gated smooth noise. wideTerrace unchanged (it reduces contour STRAIGHTNESS/salience, not tread area), plainsRough unchanged, M5 unaffected. Lowest blast radius; ONLY judgeable in-game.
2. **Fractal amplitude/face-contrast up** — k=1.15 → −19%/−17% tread area; k=1.30 → −33%/−29% but pushes summits toward the 285 clamp (re-terraces summits) and risks the rejected needle look. Gate on a summit-height histogram.

**Measured NON-fixes → do-not-retry ledger at WS completion:** symmetric hi-freq detail (±0.005), dither-before-floor (worse), amplitude REDUCTION (backfires: 0.22→0.33-0.41). Core lesson for the ledger: *tread area on a slope is conserved under quantization of any equally-smooth field — only steeper gradients or contour-breaking reduce the visual.*

### M18-S — the staircase metric (OWNER-SPECIFIED detection method, 2026-07-12 — this is the metric of record for WS1)

The owner's detection insight: read raw Y-sequences out of a large generated zone's heightmap and classify the PATTERN, because the *shape of the delta sequence* separates the three cases —
- **Terracing (the defect):** periodic gentle-run → small-jump → gentle-run alternation, e.g. `55, 56, 57, 52, 53, 54, …` — treads of near-flat/±1 steps broken by short risers, repeating.
- **Sheer mountain face (desired):** sustained large per-block drops, e.g. `128, 120, 118, 110, 98` — no gentle interludes.
- **Cliff/bluff (allowed):** an ISOLATED huge jump amid otherwise-gentle ground, e.g. `120, 119, 64` — no periodicity.

**Formal definition (implement in `biome-metrics.mjs`; calibrate in P0):**
1. Sample Y-sequences at step 1 from large zones (≥512² per seed): straight X- and Z-transects PLUS steepest-descent fall-lines seeded from high-R starts (R ≥ 0.6, reusing the sheerness gate's threshold) (both matter — warp breaks periodicity along straight transects specifically, so the metric must include them to credit the warp lever).
2. Compute the delta sequence; segment into **GENTLE runs** (consecutive |Δ| ≤ 1, length ≥ 3) and **JUMP events** (|Δ| ≥ 2 over one or two samples, magnitude ≤ 8 — jumps > 8 are cliff-class, never terrace risers).
3. A **staircase window** = any sliding 32-block window containing **≥ 3 alternations** GENTLE→JUMP with all jumps in the same sign, AND window net |ΔY| ≥ 12 (a real slope — excludes flat land where gentle steps are legitimate).
4. `staircaseIndex` = staircase-flagged samples ÷ samples in all windows with net |ΔY| ≥ 12. The cliff exemption is inherent (an isolated `120,119,64` jump can't produce ≥3 alternations); plains are excluded by the net-slope floor.
5. Companion `sheernessIndex` = P10/P50/P90 of |Δ| over mountain-face windows (macro slope ≥ 0.5 over ±16, R ≥ 0.6) — fixes should move P50 UP or hold it (mountains should read sheer), never down.

**P0 calibration requirement:** the detector MUST flag the known-terraced flank (seed 1337, center −175,162 — evidence `tools/scratch/terrace-flank-1337.png`) with a high staircaseIndex AND score known-sheer faces + isolated cliffs low. Tune the four thresholds (gentle ≤1, jump 2–8, ≥3 alternations, net ≥12) against ASCII transects until the classification matches the eyeball, then FREEZE them and record baselines per seed. wideTerrace (area) and plainsRough (blast-radius guard) stay as secondary/guard metrics.

**Phases:** P0 harness promotion (implement **M18-S staircaseIndex + sheernessIndex** per the spec above, plus wideTerrace/plainsRough from scratch, into `biome-metrics.mjs`; calibrate + freeze thresholds; record baselines ×3 seeds) → P1 implement warp behind a tunable (`TERRACE_WARP_*` keys, zero = off = byte-identical; injected; REGISTRY_KEYS lockstep) → P2 measure: adopted lever must cut `staircaseIndex` ≥ 20% on ≥2 of 3 seeds with `sheernessIndex` P50 not decreasing and plainsRough within +0.002 — then in-game A/B eyeball (OD1: warp first; amplitude-up k≈1.15 only if the eyeball still disappoints, gated on the summit histogram) → adopted lever(s) join **Bump A**.
**Worker parity:** warp lives inside `terrainSurface` knob region (injected, main-only edit). **Gates:** M18-S (primary), wideTerrace/plainsRough (guards), M3/M4/M5 unchanged, full stack, eyeball.

# WS2 — BIOME_STYLE enablement (biome terrain character)

**Cost facts:** styles-active pays per-column `classifyBiome` inside `terrainSurface`: 2 genuinely new 3-octave fbms (T, H) + REDUNDANT recomputes of C (continentalness: 4-oct warp + 3-oct fbm) and E (3-oct) that `terrainSurface` already computed for its own knobs. Measured 2.28× on non-cache callers; tree placement alone makes hundreds of `blendedHeight` calls per chunk (per-column scan + spacing tournament + `isTreeSiteViable` ring), all unamortized.

**Approach (two cuts, in order):**
1. **Eliminate the redundancy (pure, no caching):** add an internal `classifyBiomeFromFields(T, H, Cn, R, outWeights)` — the softmax core taking precomputed fields — and have `terrainSurface` pass its own C/E-derived Cn/R, computing only T/H fresh. `classifyBiome(gx,gz,out)` becomes a thin wrapper (computes all four, calls the core) so every existing caller/injection/seam export is unchanged. Estimated: removes the redundant C+E recompute — ~10 of the classifier's ~16 noise-octaves per column (**continentalHeight alone is 7**: a 4-oct domain-warp base + a 3-oct erosion fbm; erosion R adds 3; T+H are the only 6 kept fresh) — re-measure; expect ≈1.5-1.6×. (An earlier draft undercounted continentalHeight as 4 octaves, giving "~7 of ~13"; the residual T+H=6 is correct either way.)
2. **Amortize T/H behind a quantized-coordinate module-scope memo** ONLY if cut 1 misses the gate (new architecture — no existing precedent for module-scope pure-function memos outside the cache builder; precedent for Maps: `treePositionsCache` cap-512, `biomeCellCache` cap-10K w/ distance eviction). T/H vary over ~370-400 blocks; a step-8 quantized memo is visually lossless for STYLE (not for height — never quantize R/C). AUDIT FLAG: the memo must be deterministic-read (same value regardless of call order) — trivially true for a pure-function memo, but it must ALSO be emitted/declared in the worker module scope.
3. **Wire `soilDepth`** — currently accumulated by `styleBlend` with ZERO consumers (grep-verified). Consumer site: `generateTerrainPass`'s subsurface depth-1..3 layer logic; flag-style-gated; LOCKSTEP with `isTreeSoilSurface` if it changes any surface-material outcome.
4. **Tune the biases** (the actual point): per-biome character targets — plains: negative roughness/ridge (flatter, calmer), swamp: negative baseBias (sits lower, wetter feel), forests/longwoods: mild warp+roughness (organic), hills: positive ridgeMix nudge (billowy→rolling), mountains: 0 (already emergent from R). Tuning loop = M-table + hillshade pairs + transects; every bias step re-runs M3/M4/M5 (style CAN shift seams — τ interplay) and the cost gate.

**Cost gate:** styles-active ≤ **1.35×** flag-ON-zero-styles on the per-column benchmark (Phase-2 method), AND spawn pre-gen wall-clock within +5% in-game. **Phases:** P0 re-bench harness → P1 refactor (cut 1; zero-style byte-identity by direct diff) → P2 soilDepth consumer → P3 tuning (Node) → P4 in-game eyeball → joins **Bump A**. **Parity:** all injected; new tunables → emission + schema + REGISTRY_KEYS same-commit.

# WS3 — forceSingleBiome shape-forcing (Q6)

Single-site: `terrainSurface`'s relief sourcing gains a forced branch — when `biomeDriven && worldConfig.forceSingleBiome` is a `BIOME_ID_ORDER` member, `relief = BIOME_CENTROIDS[forced].r` (constant) instead of `spline(SPLINE_RELIEF, E)`. `classifyBiome` already one-hots (P3-R3). Consistency: also clamp the `cornerR`/`climCache` R-plane in `precalculateTerrainCaches`' forced case (currently unread downstream, but don't leave a disagreeing cache). AUDIT NOTE: forced-flat relief means a forced-plains world is TRULY flat-ish (amplitude = 0.12·RELIEF_AMPLITUDE) — that is the point (owner Q6: shape matches skin); the create-world preview picks it up automatically via `blendedHeight`.
**Worker parity:** both edit sites (`terrainSurface`, `precalculateTerrainCaches`) are injected `terrainFuncs` — main-only edit, injection markers intact (no hand-maintained worker copy to sync). The forced branch reads `worldConfig.forceSingleBiome` which is already baked into the worker at pool creation.
**Gates:** forced-world probe (new mini-check: for each of 6 biomes forced, sampled relief variance ≈ 0 and heights within the biome's expected band); unforced worlds byte-identical (direct diff); browser suite (the "honors forceSingleBiome" test stays green). Ships with **Bump A** (only forced worlds change; still a terrain-output change for them).

# WS4 — Larger climate regions

**Facts:** autocorr ≈ ⅓·(1/freq), measured T 395/369/407 · H 324/338/370 · C 151/160/166 · R 272/407/315. Doubling = T 0.0009→0.00045, H 0.0011→0.00055, R(erosion) 0.0011→0.00055, C 0.002/0.004→0.001/0.002. **C's frequencies are bare literals** — not behind `paramFreq`, so today's Biome Size slider never touched them; promote all retuned frequencies to `GEN_TUNABLES` keys (`FREQ_TEMPERATURE`, `FREQ_HUMIDITY`, `FREQ_EROSION`, `FREQ_CONTINENTAL_BASE`, `FREQ_CONTINENTAL_EROSION`) with schema rows + emission + REGISTRY_KEYS, defaults = CURRENT values (byte-identical), retuned values flip with **Bump A**.
**Knock-ons to re-gate (all in the existing harness):** M2 targets re-derived at the new operating point (the ⅓ ratio was only validated near current freqs — REMEASURE, don't extrapolate); M7 should improve; M5 must re-verify (marginal distribution unchanged in theory, but `SPLINE_RELIEF` was calibrated against current R statistics); M4 seam (transition widths scale with 1/freq — τ likely fine, verify); M6 if C is touched (OD6). **Phases:** P0 prototype both OD6 variants (Node + hillshades at 2048+ extents so the larger regions are visible) → owner picks → P1 tunable promotion (byte-identical) → retune joins **Bump A**.

# WS6 — Hydrological rivers (the big one)

**Prototyped design (scripts: `tools/scratch/_hydro_proto.mjs`, `_hydro_continuity.mjs`, `_hydro_depression.mjs` — the decisive probe):**
- **Architecture:** global integer lattice, cell = 32 blocks (step-8 has 78 spurious noise pits fragmenting rivers; step-32 has 1; step-64 too coarse). Regions of 1024 blocks bound work and cache polylines; they NEVER change values — every trace is a pure function of its spring cell: greedy 8-neighbor descent on `getPreRiverHeight` sampled at lattice points (integer coords, exact compares, fixed tie-break order — no float accumulation; the gradient-descent tracer design was abandoned for exactly this float-accumulation reason — its vestige `getLocalSlope` is tombstoned `[TOMBSTONE TER-11]` ~40908, labeled "never-built gradient-descent river tracer").
- **The make-or-break:** raw greedy reaches ocean only 24-50% (pits). **Pit-centered flood-and-spill breaching** (Barnes-style priority-flood per pit, bounded by a 32-cell halo FROM THE PIT — not from any region edge, which is what makes it seam-free by construction): **100% ocean connectivity, 0 halo-failures, ×3 seeds**; max observed basin 26 cells < 32 halo.
- **Costs (measured):** region build 10-20 ms (amortized <0.1% of chunk gen across 4096 chunks/region; LRU-cached module-scope Map, declared BOTH main + worker); per-column distance query 0.42 µs naive = **0.96× the current ribbon** (indexed variant 1.32× scales better); `getPreRiverHeight` 1.10 µs dominates everything as always.
- **v1 features:** springs (pre-river height >95 + local-ridge test, ~45/region; humidity-gating is a 1-line tuning follow-up), flow accumulation → width at confluences (free during tracing), delta reuse (`getDeltaFingerFactor` already ocean-gated). **Deferred to v2:** lakes at true minima (needs persistent water-plane — different problem class), fjords (OD5).
- **Integration:** `riverFactorAt(gx,gz,seed,preHeight,widthMult)` keeps `getRiverFactor`'s exact signature and 0→1 shaping so `applyRiverCarve`/`precalculateTerrainCaches`/valley-band callers are UNCHANGED; behind `WORLD_CONFIG.hydroRivers` (false = ribbon, byte-identical). New injected functions: `hydroRegionOf`, `hydroLatticeH`, `buildHydroRegion`, `floodSpill`, `riverFactorAt`; new tunables `HYDRO_REGION/STEP/HALO/SPRING_H/FLOW_WIDTH_*` (emission + schema + REGISTRY_KEYS).
- **New gates:** **M14 → GATING** (≥99% springs reach ocean; measured 100%) · **M15** monotonic-descent invariant (non-increasing except bounded spill rises) · **M16** cross-region byte-identical polylines (the seam gate) · **M17** basin extent ≤ halo. M8 unchanged. Spawn-region pre-warm (one ~20 ms build before the pre-gen spiral) guards the main-thread-bound spawn path.
- **Phases:** P0 promote prototype metrics into the harness → P1 plumbing flag-OFF byte-identical → P2 flag-ON implementation, all gates → P3 in-game tuning/eyeball (rivers around mountains, deltas at coasts) → P4 flip = **Bump B**. Ribbon stays as the flag-OFF fallback (do not delete; WS7/OD4 owns its eventual fate).

## Sequencing & dependency graph

```
WS3 (tiny, independent) ─┐
WS2 (styles)            ─┼─→ Bump A flip ──→ soak ──→ WS7 (OD4)
WS4 (regions, OD6)      ─┤        ↑
WS5 (biomes, OD2)       ─┤        │
WS1 (terracing, OD1)    ─┘   (single regen)
WS6 (hydro, OD5) ────────────→ Bump B flip ──────────↗
```
Recommended execution order: **WS3 → WS2 → WS5 → WS4 → WS1 → [Bump A] → WS6 → [Bump B] → soak → WS7**. WS2 precedes WS5 because desert/tundra/snowy_peaks want style rows at birth; WS4 precedes WS1's final eyeball because region scale changes which slopes you look at; WS6 is independent and can proceed in parallel by a separate hand-off after its P0. Each workstream's phases follow the runbook: audit → implement → verify (cheapest first) → as-built, one phase per hand-off.

`classifyBiomeFromFields`, `TERRACE_WARP_*`, `FREQ_TEMPERATURE`, `FREQ_HUMIDITY`, `FREQ_EROSION`, `FREQ_CONTINENTAL_BASE`, `FREQ_CONTINENTAL_EROSION`, `hydroRivers`, `hydroRegionOf`, `hydroLatticeH`, `buildHydroRegion`, `floodSpill`, `riverFactorAt`, `hydroRegionCache`, `HYDRO_REGION`, `HYDRO_STEP`, `HYDRO_HALO`, `HYDRO_SPRING_H`, `FLOW_WIDTH_SCALE`, biome names `desert`/`tundra`/`snowy_peaks`, metrics M15–M20. All-new as of build 2026-07-12.4 (zero occurrences verified for the hydro/terrace names during investigation; re-verify at each audit).

# WS8 — Coastal erosion (fjords, cliffs/bluffs, flow-driven deltas) (DONE — see As-built)

**Status: DONE, 2026-07-14, build `2026-07-14.3`, TGV 42 — see the "WS8 — DONE" As-built entry below for gates/renders/measurements.** Draft/audit history preserved below for context. Originally written against live `voxEx.html` at `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` 40, build `2026-07-14.1` (post WS6-P8). **Opus audit 2026-07-14 re-verified every anchor and Before snippet against the post-WS6-P10 build (`2026-07-14.2`, TGV 41, ~52.8K lines):** every Before snippet is still VERBATIM-CORRECT; line hints drifted (WS6-P10 inserted ~77 lines inside `buildHydroRegion`, shifting everything after ~41800 by +77, and a few earlier plumbing lines by +10). The inline hints in F1/F2/F3 below have been refreshed to the post-P10 values; a full old→new correction table is in the audit record at the end of this section. **Still re-verify against the then-current build before editing** — WS8's own implementation, or any WS landing between now and then, will drift these again.

## Motivation

**Owner request (verbatim, 2026-07-14):** "Can we apply the same hydro erosion idea to ocean/lake coasts? I ask because the rivers and oceans are now very obviously created with different methods. and this could also open the door to better natural things like river delta's into oceans and fjords and cliffs and bluffs."

The observation is correct and already on record in this CCR's own fact table: rivers (post-WS6) are traced, routed, flow-accumulated erosion features — springs descend real terrain, flood-and-spill breaches pits, channel width responds to accumulated flow. Oceans are still exactly what they were before WS6 ever existed: a stationary two-threshold domain-warped noise field (`getOceanFactor`, unchanged since before this CCR) with a single hardcoded depth profile (`getOceanDepth`) and zero relationship to relief, flow, or the river network that empties into them. Item 5 of this CCR's own Problem/Why table recorded the measured consequence of trying to unify them naively: **"ocean CANNOT key off C[ontinentalness]: measured Pearson r ≈ −0.012" (≈ zero)** — the two systems don't just look different, they are statistically almost independent fields. WS8 does not try to unify them into one system; it makes the OCEAN side responsive to the SAME terrain signals (relief `R`, and now river flow) that already drive the river side, so the coastline reads as eroded-by-the-same-world rather than stamped-by-a-different-algorithm.

**Supersedes/absorbs prior deferrals:**
- **OD5 (WS6 scope):** "lakes + fjords defer to v2" — WS8-F1 is that v2 fjords item, now scoped concretely against the shipped hydro river system (lakes at true minima remain out of scope; a fjord under this design is a drowned RIVER valley, not a lake).
- **OD3 (ocean biome timing):** "ocean biome AFTER WS6" — WS8 does not add an ocean biome/label; it only reshapes the ocean HEIGHT profile and delta material rule via existing `oceanFactor`/`riverFactorAt` outputs. OD3's actual ocean-as-a-classified-biome item remains open and untouched by this workstream.
- The r≈-0.012 finding is the reason F2 below keys the cliff/shelf blend on **relief `R`** (the same axis the river/mountain systems already use), not on continentalness.

## F1 — Fjords (drowned river valleys)

**Mechanism.** Where a hydro channel's course crosses the coastline — defined as: the channel core (`r < 1.0` from `riverFactorAt`) sits at a `preHeight` inside a band around sea level, AND `oceanFactor < 1.0` is reachable within the channel's own valley-influence width — extend the EXISTING channel-incision bed below sea level for a tunable inland distance (`FJORD_REACH`), tapering back to the normal river-valley profile as the channel moves away from the coast. Steep-relief coasts (`reliefParam` high at the crossing) get deeper/longer fjord arms (mirrors real fjords: glacially-carved valleys in mountainous terrain); low-relief river mouths keep today's shallow, wide estuary/delta look untouched.

**Exact edit site** — `applyRiverCarve`'s existing FULL-canyon-carve block (grep anchor `function applyRiverCarve`, function header at line 40401; the `if (inRiver || inDeltaFinger)` block opens at line 40449 and the carve depth `getRiverDepth`/`riverBed` is computed at lines 40459-40460):

Before (verbatim, live file):
```js
                if (inRiver || inDeltaFinger) {
                    // cStr fades the CHANNEL carve over preHeight 82..95: rivers
                    // shallow out and end as a dry ravine head high in the hills
                    // (natural stream source) rather than hitting a wall. Water
                    // persists to ~preHeight 112-115 because the valley floor is
                    // already near sea level by then.
                    const cStr = 1 - smoothstep(82, 95, preHeight);
                    if (cStr > 0) {
                        const waterFactor = inDeltaFinger ? Math.min(r, deltaFinger) : r;

                        const riverDepth = getRiverDepth(waterFactor, gx, gz, seed, oceanFactor);
                        const riverBed = WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel - riverDepth;
```

After (sketch — the additive term only; `cStr`/`waterFactor` computation above is unchanged):
```js
                if (inRiver || inDeltaFinger) {
                    const cStr = 1 - smoothstep(82, 95, preHeight);
                    if (cStr > 0) {
                        const waterFactor = inDeltaFinger ? Math.min(r, deltaFinger) : r;

                        let riverDepth = getRiverDepth(waterFactor, gx, gz, seed, oceanFactor);
                        // WS8-F1: fjord extension — only reachable near the coast, where a
                        // valley-band query (widthMult=3, already computed above as vfRaw/vf
                        // for the valley-depression block) indicates open ocean is close AND
                        // this column sits inside the coastal band around sea level.
                        if (waterFactor < 0.9 && preHeight < WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel + FJORD_COAST_BAND
                            && oceanFactor < FJORD_OCEAN_GATE) {
                            const rC = reliefParam(gx, gz); // 0 flat .. 1 mountainous
                            const fjordDepthAdd = FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE * Math.max(0, rC - FJORD_RELIEF_MIN)
                                / (1 - FJORD_RELIEF_MIN);
                            riverDepth += fjordDepthAdd * (1 - oceanFactor); // tapers inland
                        }
                        const riverBed = WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel - riverDepth;
```

**cStr interplay — verified sound (audit).** The fjord depth add lives inside the existing `if (cStr > 0)` guard, where `cStr = 1 - smoothstep(82, 95, preHeight)` fades the channel carve out over `preHeight` 82..95. The fjord-eligible band ceiling is `preHeight < WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel + FJORD_COAST_BAND` = `< 75` at the default — which sits ENTIRELY below where `cStr` begins fading (82), so `cStr === 1` throughout the fjord band and the added depth applies at full carve strength. WS6-P7's "cStr zeroes carving above preHeight 95" fact therefore does not undercut F1: fjord arms only ever exist at `preHeight < 75`, deep in the full-carve zone. (This holds for any `FJORD_COAST_BAND` below ~22; a user dialing it above ~22 would push the fjord band into the cStr fade and get progressively weaker fjord depth near the top of the band — a graceful degradation, not a defect, but noted since the tested range tops out at 40.)

`FJORD_REACH` (the "how far inland" distance named in the assignment) is realized here as the existing `oceanFactor` gradient itself rather than a new distance computation — `oceanFactor` already smoothsteps from 0 (open ocean) to 1 (fully inland) over the SAME coastal blend band F2 touches below, so `(1 - oceanFactor)` is a free, already-computed proxy for "how close to the coast" with no new distance query. This is a deliberate reuse, not a placeholder; if in-game tuning shows the existing ocean blend band is too narrow/wide for a convincing fjord arm, `OCEAN_WARP_AMP`/the two `OCEAN_THRESHOLD_*` tunables (already exposed) are the levers, not a new distance field.

**New tunables** (proposed `GEN_TUNABLES` section: `'Coastal Erosion'`, all `ui: 'both'`):
- `FJORD_COAST_BAND` (default 15, tested [5, 40]) — label "Fjord Coast Band (blk)": how far above sea level (in preHeight) the fjord-eligible band around the coastline extends. Note: "Height window around the coastline where a river-crossing-the-coast can extend its channel bed into a fjord. TERRAIN-CHANGING."
- `FJORD_OCEAN_GATE` (default 0.6, tested [0.3, 0.9]) — label "Fjord Ocean Gate": oceanFactor must be below this for the fjord extension to engage (0 = must be fully in open ocean territory before any fjord depth is added; higher = fjords start forming further inland). TERRAIN-CHANGING.
- `FJORD_RELIEF_MIN` (default 0.5, tested [0.3, 0.7]) — label "Fjord Relief Threshold": relief (`reliefParam`) below this produces NO fjord depth (low-relief coasts stay estuaries); above it, depth ramps to `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE` at `R=1`. TERRAIN-CHANGING.
- `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE` (default 25, tested [10, 45]) — label "Fjord Depth (blk)": maximum extra channel-bed depth added at a fully-qualifying (high-relief, coastal-band, near-ocean) crossing, on top of the existing `getRiverDepth` bed. TERRAIN-CHANGING.

**Honest risk:** the fjord extension and F3's delta widening (below) are both gated on the SAME site (a river crossing the coast) with opposite intents — a fjord DEEPENS the bed, a delta WIDENS/shallows it (`getDeltaFingerFactor`/`getRiverDepth`'s existing delta-shallowing branch already does the latter). They are not mutually exclusive today: `FJORD_RELIEF_MIN` is the discriminator (high-relief mouths fjord, low-relief mouths delta), but a coast that transitions from mountainous to flat over a short run could show BOTH behaviors fighting at the same crossing. This needs an explicit P0 probe before implementation — flagged, not resolved, here.

**Concrete P0 resolution experiment (auditor-specified).** Against the extracted functions (`buildTerrainApi`), sample every river-crosses-coast column across ≥3 seeds (a column where `riverFactorAt < 1.0` AND `getOceanFactor` is in the coastal transition band `(0, 0.999)`). For each, record `rC = reliefParam(gx,gz)`, whether F1's fjord branch WOULD fire (`waterFactor < 0.9 && preHeight < seaLevel+FJORD_COAST_BAND && oceanFactor < FJORD_OCEAN_GATE`), and whether `getDeltaFingerFactor(...) < 1.0` fires. Compute the OVERLAP population = columns where BOTH fire. **Decision rule:** if overlap ≤ ~2% of all coastal-crossing columns on all seeds, the `FJORD_RELIEF_MIN` gate separates the two cleanly — ship as drafted. If overlap exceeds that, make them mutually exclusive BY CONSTRUCTION: gate the delta-finger call so it only engages below the fjord relief threshold — pass `rC` into `getDeltaFingerFactor` and early-return `1.0` when `rC >= FJORD_RELIEF_MIN` (or, equivalently, skip `inDeltaFinger` in `applyRiverCarve` on high-relief columns). Confirm the chosen path visually with two `tools/terrain-probe.mjs hillshade` renders: one at a known mountainous river mouth (fjord expected, no delta fingers) and one at a known flat river mouth (delta expected, no fjord deepening). This experiment is a required P0 exit gate, not optional.

## F2 — Cliff/bluff coast profile

**Mechanism.** In `computePreRiverHeight`'s ocean blend, the land→seafloor transition profile becomes relief-driven instead of a fixed smoothstep width. Sample `reliefParam(gx, gz)` at the column: high `R` → sharp cliff profile (land holds its height until very close to the waterline, then a wave-cut-notch-style rapid drop over a narrow `oceanFactor` band); low `R` → the CURRENT smooth shelf (unchanged, beach-compatible). This must (a) keep the existing M6 land/ocean AREA split within its established gate (the profile change must not add or remove land, only reshape the transition curve), and (b) stay coherent with the beach material gate — cliff coasts must NOT get beach sand, which requires an `R`-aware exception mirrored into `isTreeSoilSurface` per the Lockstep Registry.

**Exact edit site** — `computePreRiverHeight`'s ocean-apply block (grep anchor `function computePreRiverHeight`, function header at line 40351; the `// 4. Apply oceans first` block is at lines 40379-40386):

Before (verbatim, live file):
```js
                // 4. Apply oceans first (larger scale feature)
                const oceanFactor = getOceanFactor(gx, gz, seed);
                if (oceanFactor < 1.0) {
                    const oceanDepth = getOceanDepth(oceanFactor, gx, gz, seed);
                    const oceanFloor = WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel - oceanDepth;
                    const bank = oceanFactor * oceanFactor * (3 - 2 * oceanFactor);
                    finalHeight = lerpValue(oceanFloor, finalHeight, bank);
                }
```

After (sketch — `bank` gains an R-dependent exponent instead of the fixed smoothstep cubic; everything else, including `oceanFloor`/`oceanDepth`, is unchanged):
```js
                // 4. Apply oceans first (larger scale feature)
                const oceanFactor = getOceanFactor(gx, gz, seed);
                if (oceanFactor < 1.0) {
                    const oceanDepth = getOceanDepth(oceanFactor, gx, gz, seed);
                    const oceanFloor = WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel - oceanDepth;
                    // WS8-F2: relief-driven transition sharpness. rC=0 -> current smoothstep
                    // (CLIFF_SHARPNESS exponent floor of 1, byte-identical cubic). rC=1 -> a
                    // steep power curve that holds `finalHeight` near-full until oceanFactor
                    // is very close to 1, then drops sharply (bank rockets toward 0 late).
                    const rC = reliefParam(gx, gz);
                    // AUDIT FIX: guard the denominator. The create-world/editor UI accepts any
                    // finite tunable value (no clamp), so a user CAN set CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN = 1.0,
                    // which makes (1 - CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN) === 0 and turns cliffT into a 0/0 = NaN
                    // (or -Infinity for rC<1) that propagates all the way to a NaN terrain height.
                    // Math.max(1e-6, ...) keeps the divide finite; the numerator's own Math.max(0,...)
                    // + the outer clamp then still deliver cliffT === 0 for the CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN >= 1
                    // "cliffs effectively off" case without any NaN.
                    const cliffDen = Math.max(1e-6, 1 - CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN);
                    const cliffT = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, Math.max(0, rC - CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN) / cliffDen));
                    const sharpness = 1 + cliffT * (CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX - 1);
                    const ofP = Math.pow(oceanFactor, sharpness);
                    const bank = ofP * ofP * (3 - 2 * ofP); // reuse ofP; smoothstep of the sharpened factor
                    finalHeight = lerpValue(oceanFloor, finalHeight, bank);
                }
```
(At `cliffT = 0`, `sharpness = 1` and `Math.pow(oceanFactor, 1) === oceanFactor`, making `bank === oceanFactor*oceanFactor*(3-2*oceanFactor)` — byte-identical to today's cubic smoothstep. The exponent generalization is chosen specifically so the default/low-relief case reduces exactly to the current formula, not merely approximates it. **AUDIT NOTE:** the ORIGINAL sketch computed `Math.pow(oceanFactor, sharpness)` twice and wrote `3 - 2 * Math.pow(...)` — algebraically the same but redundant; hoisted to a single `ofP` so the byte-identity argument is textually obvious and the pow isn't paid twice.)

**Material-gate tie-in** — the beach/shore gate in `generateTerrainPass` (grep anchor `--- BEACH/SHORE (CCR-TERRAIN-011`, currently lines 43253-43267) must gain an R exception so cliff coasts don't sand:

Before (verbatim, live file, the condition only):
```js
                                    else if (((oceanFactor < 0.999 && !isSwampCol) || riverFactor < 0.7) &&
                                             worldY < WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel + 2 + surfaceNoise * 1.5 &&
                                             worldY >= WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel - 1) {
                                        id = SAND;
                                    }
```
After (sketch — adds `!isCoastCliffCol` to the ocean arm only, mirroring how `!isSwampCol` already scopes the same arm; the river arm is untouched since cliffs are an OCEAN-coast concept):
```js
                                    else if (((oceanFactor < 0.999 && !isSwampCol && !isCoastCliffCol) || riverFactor < 0.7) &&
                                             worldY < WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel + 2 + surfaceNoise * 1.5 &&
                                             worldY >= WORLD_DIMS.seaLevel - 1) {
                                        id = SAND;
                                    }
```
`isCoastCliffCol` reads the R plane of `climCache` (plane index 3, ALREADY computed and bilinear-interpolated from a coarse corner grid inside `precalculateTerrainCaches` for the biome-driven classifier — see the AUDIT NOTE below) — cheap, no new per-column noise call, same pattern as the existing `isSwampCol`/`isDesertCol`/`isTundraCol` local `const`s a few lines above this gate (grep anchor `_colBiomeId`, currently lines 43207-43210). **Two AUDIT FIXES to the naive sketch:**

```js
// AUDIT FIX 1 (null-guard): climCache is NULL on the legacy biomeDrivenTerrain=false
//   escape hatch (built only inside the `if (worldConfig.biomeDrivenTerrain)` block ~line
//   42926) — reading climCache[...] unguarded there is a TypeError. Mirror _colBiomeId's guard.
// AUDIT FIX 2 (scope): `planeStride` is a const LOCAL to the climCache-build block (line 42927),
//   NOT in scope at the beach gate (~43263). Use `chunkSize * chunkSize` directly (chunkSize IS
//   in scope here — the same value idx is computed from).
const isCoastCliffCol = (worldConfig.biomeDrivenTerrain && caches.climCache)
    ? caches.climCache[idx + 3 * chunkSize * chunkSize] > CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN
    : false;
```

**Do not confuse this local with the EXISTING `isCliff` const** (currently line 43145, `maxSlope >= CLIFF_SLOPE` — a mountain-flank slope concept, unrelated) — name collision checked (grep of the whole file: zero prior `isCoastCliffCol`/`FJORD`/`CLIFF_RELIEF`/`CLIFF_SHARPNESS`/`DELTA_FLOW`/`COAST` occurrences), `isCoastCliffCol` and all new tunable names are free. LOCKSTEP: `isTreeSoilSurface` (grep anchor `function isTreeSoilSurface`, currently lines 5951-5965) mirrors this exact rule today for `isSwampCol`; it needs the same `!isCoastCliffCol`-equivalent addition, but `isTreeSoilSurface` has no `climCache` available (it's a standalone per-column probe, not fed cached planes) — it would need its OWN `reliefParam(gx, gz)` call. **AUDIT — mirror R-source divergence (residual risk):** the cascade reads bilinear-interpolated COARSE-grid climCache R while `isTreeSoilSurface` would read EXACT per-column `reliefParam` — the two can disagree by a hair right at the `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN` threshold on boundary columns, exactly the lockstep-divergence class the Registry warns about. Practical impact is low (high-relief cliff columns are steep/high and the elevation ladder rejects them as tree soil regardless of the sand branch), but P0/P1 must either (a) accept and DOCUMENT the coarse/exact boundary mismatch as tolerable, or (b) have the cascade ALSO call `reliefParam` directly (dropping the climCache reuse, paying the fbm cost in the cascade too) so both mirrors read the identical R source. Recommend (a) unless a P0 render shows visible sand/soil speckling at cliff-coast tree lines.

**AUDIT NOTE — cost honesty (two different R sources, deliberately) — RE-VERIFIED:** F2's HEIGHT-shaping use of `reliefParam()` inside `computePreRiverHeight` is a **fresh, uncached fbm call** (`erosionParam`→`erosionParamD`, a real noise cost) added to a function that already runs once per column during `precalculateTerrainCaches`'s main loop (confirmed at lines 42891-42910: `computePreRiverHeight` at 42896, `riverFactorAt` at 42899, `applyRiverCarve` at 42902, all same column) — this loop runs BEFORE `climCache`'s coarse corner-grid R plane is built (confirmed: that happens in the `if (worldConfig.biomeDrivenTerrain)` block at lines 42926+, a later pass of the same function), so `computePreRiverHeight` cannot reuse `climCache` even though it exists in the same function — they are sequential passes, not concurrent. The MATERIAL-GATE's use of R (`isCoastCliffCol` above) CAN reuse `climCache` because `generateTerrainPass` runs after `precalculateTerrainCaches` has fully populated it. Net: F2 pays one new fbm-cost `reliefParam()` call per column for the height profile, but zero new cost for the material-gate exception. This is the same "R is cheap XOR reused, never both automatically" tension noted in WS1/WS3's own use of `reliefParam` — P0 must MEASURE this against the ~1.10µs `getPreRiverHeight`-dominates-everything baseline WS6 already established, not assume it's negligible.

**AUDIT — cost-mitigation levers if P0's measurement fails the gate (in preference order):** (1) **Restrict the fbm call to columns that can actually be a coast** — F2's height-shaping only matters where `oceanFactor < 1.0` (already inside that `if`), so the `reliefParam()` call is ALREADY paid only on ocean-blend columns, not every land column; open-ocean-free chunks pay nothing. Quantify what fraction of columns hit the `oceanFactor < 1.0` branch before assuming worst-case per-column cost. (2) **Reorder the coarse cornerR grid build to BEFORE the main per-column loop** and have `computePreRiverHeight` read the bilinear-interpolated R for free — cleaner than a quantized memo, but flag-ON only (no climCache on the legacy path, so it needs a `reliefParam` fallback there) and it swaps exact per-column R for smoothed coarse-grid R in the height profile (acceptable — arguably desirable, a smoother coastline — but a genuine output change to characterize, not byte-identical). (3) The WS2-style quantized-coordinate module-scope R memo the draft already names, as the last resort. Levers (1) and (2) are new here; do not jump to the memo without checking (1) first.

**New tunables** (same `'Coastal Erosion'` section):
- `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN` (default 0.55, tested [0.35, 0.75]) — label "Cliff Relief Threshold": relief below this keeps the current smooth-shelf coast; above it, the transition sharpens toward `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX` (also gates the beach-sand exception, same value, single source of truth for "is this a cliff coast"). TERRAIN-CHANGING.
- `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX` (default 6, tested [3, 12]) — label "Cliff Sharpness (max)": exponent applied to `oceanFactor` at full relief; higher = a more abrupt wave-cut-notch-style drop right at the waterline. 1 = no sharpening (today's behavior). TERRAIN-CHANGING.

## F3 — Flow-driven deltas

**Mechanism.** `getDeltaFingerFactor` currently sizes its fingers from pure coordinate noise (`fingerScale1/2/3`) modulated only by `oceanFactor` proximity — it has NO idea whether the river feeding this delta is a 12-block trunk or a 2-block trickle, so a tiny stream gets the same finger-splitting treatment as a major river. Drive delta presence/scale by the river's actual flow accumulation at its mouth (`bestFlow`, already computed inside `riverFactorAt`'s bucket search but currently thrown away — grep anchor `function riverFactorAt`, `let bestD2 = Infinity, bestFlow = 1;` at line 42254, used only to compute `flowBoost`/width at line 42280 and never returned).

**Exposing `bestFlow` without a hot-path cost when unused — AUDITOR DESIGN CALL.** The assignment asks to avoid re-paying the bucket search AND to weigh the scratch-vs-sibling-function trade-off and its same-frame-ordering fragility. Three designs considered:
1. **Sibling function `riverFlowAt`** — duplicates the entire 3×3-neighborhood bucket search (lines 42255-42277) to return `bestFlow` alone. Rejected: this is the EXACT class of cost WS6-P7 already measured and rejected for a similar ask (`riverFactorAt`'s own comment on the `haveElevation` fallback, lines 42188-42201, records a fallback attempt measured at up to 3.02× cost and calls it out as too expensive) — a second full query is the same mistake. (Note: it would re-scan segments only, not rebuild the cached region, so it is cheaper than the getPreRiverHeight fallback — but it re-pays the bucket loop for EVERY column, since `getDeltaFingerFactor` is called per-column, and that is exactly the per-column re-query WS6-P7's cost gate exists to prevent.)
2. **Module-scope out-param scratch, read across the caller boundary** (the draft's original recommendation) — a `const _riverFlowScratch = { flow: 1 };` at module scope, written on every `riverFactorAt` return, read by `applyRiverCarve` whenever it computes `deltaFinger`. Correct in TODAY's call graph (verified: `precalculateTerrainCaches` calls `riverFactorAt` at line 42899 then `applyRiverCarve` at 42902 for the SAME column with no other `riverFactorAt` in between; the `blendedHeight` path calls `applyRiverCarve` with `riverFactor` undefined, so `applyRiverCarve`'s OWN internal `riverFactorAt` at line 40412 sets the scratch just before `getDeltaFingerFactor` at 40414 reads it). But it reads a module GLOBAL across a caller boundary at an unpredictable time — a future refactor that interleaves ANY `riverFactorAt` call for a different column between the caller's query and `applyRiverCarve`'s read silently corrupts delta sizing, with NO gate catching it (M22/M23 measure aggregate coherence, not per-column flow provenance). **Partially rejected** — keep the scratch mechanism, drop the cross-boundary read.
3. **Scratch as zero-alloc transport OUT of `riverFactorAt`, but every READ adjacent to its WRITE, value threaded as an explicit parameter across caller boundaries (RECOMMENDED — auditor's call).** The scratch is still needed (returning an object from the hot `riverFactorAt` would allocate/violate the no-closure rule; `_riverFlowScratch` mirrors the existing `_lightCtxScratch` idiom — grep anchor `_lightCtxScratch`, module-scope decl at line 42572). Rules that remove the fragility by construction:
   - `riverFactorAt` sets `_riverFlowScratch.flow` immediately before EVERY return. There are **FIVE** returns (the draft's original "both early-outs" enumeration was incomplete — audit found five): line 42185 `return getRiverFactor(...)` (ribbon dispatch, hydroRivers OFF) → **write 1** (ribbon has no flow concept; 1 = neutral = byte-identical delta); line 42278 `return 1.0` (no polylines in window) → **write 1**; line 42299 `return 1.0` (`halfWidth < 0.01`) → **write 1**; line 42301 `return 1.0` (`dist >= halfWidth`, just outside channel but polylines existed) → **write `bestFlow`** (a near-mouth delta finger benefits from knowing the trunk size even when the column itself is just outside the channel core; and since `DELTA_FLOW_SCALE=0` staging makes the multiplier exactly 1 regardless, this choice is free of byte-identity consequences and only matters once flipped); line 42303 `return t*t*(3-2*t)` (in-channel) → **write `bestFlow`**.
   - Every CONSUMER reads the scratch on the line IMMEDIATELY FOLLOWING its own `riverFactorAt` call, never later. In `applyRiverCarve`, add an explicit `riverFlow` parameter mirroring the existing `riverFactor` parameter: on the `blendedHeight`/undefined path, `applyRiverCarve`'s own `riverFactorAt` at line 40412 sets the scratch and the very next line reads it (`const rFlow = riverFlow !== undefined ? riverFlow : _riverFlowScratch.flow;`); on the precompute path, `precalculateTerrainCaches` reads `_riverFlowScratch.flow` on the line right after its own `riverFactorAt` at 42899 and threads it into `applyRiverCarve`'s new `riverFlow` arg at 42902.
   - Net: the flow value travels as DATA alongside `riverFactor` (exactly how `riverFactor` itself is already threaded), and every scratch read sits one line below its write. The same-frame ordering dependency the draft's design "inherited knowingly" is **removed** — a future refactor can move `applyRiverCarve` calls freely without silently mis-reading flow. Cost is one extra function parameter and one extra property read per column (identical to design 2's cost), NOT a second bucket search. Byte-identity when `DELTA_FLOW_SCALE=0` stays trivially provable (the multiplier is exactly 1 regardless of the flow value read).

**Exact consumer edit site** — `applyRiverCarve`'s delta-finger call (grep anchor `function applyRiverCarve`, header at line 40401; the `const r = ...` / `const deltaFinger = ...` block is at lines 40411-40414). Per the design call above, `applyRiverCarve`'s signature gains a `riverFlow` param alongside `riverFactor`: `function applyRiverCarve(gx, gz, seed, preHeight, oceanFactor, riverFactor, riverFlow)`.

Before (verbatim, live file, lines 40411-40414):
```js
                const r = riversOn
                    ? (riverFactor !== undefined ? riverFactor : riverFactorAt(gx, gz, seed, preHeight))
                    : 1.0;
                const deltaFinger = riversOn ? getDeltaFingerFactor(gx, gz, seed, oceanFactor) : 1.0;
```
After (sketch — flow is threaded, not read across a boundary; `getDeltaFingerFactor` gains one optional trailing param):
```js
                const r = riversOn
                    ? (riverFactor !== undefined ? riverFactor : riverFactorAt(gx, gz, seed, preHeight))
                    : 1.0;
                // WS8-F3: flow-scaled delta size. rFlow is threaded in on the precompute path
                // (precalculateTerrainCaches passes riverFlow, read from _riverFlowScratch on the
                // line right after ITS OWN riverFactorAt at 42899); on the blendedHeight/undefined
                // path, applyRiverCarve's own riverFactorAt just above (line 40412) set the scratch,
                // so we read it here — one line below its write, never across a caller boundary.
                const rFlow = riverFlow !== undefined ? riverFlow : (riversOn ? _riverFlowScratch.flow : 1);
                const deltaFinger = riversOn ? getDeltaFingerFactor(gx, gz, seed, oceanFactor, rFlow) : 1.0;
```
And at the precompute caller (`precalculateTerrainCaches`, currently lines 42899-42902):
```js
                        const rf = riversOn ? riverFactorAt(gx, gz, seed, pre.height) : 1.0;
                        const rFlow = riversOn ? _riverFlowScratch.flow : 1;   // read adjacent to the write above
                        riverCache[idx] = rf;
                        oceanCache[idx] = pre.oceanFactor;
                        const h = applyRiverCarve(gx, gz, seed, pre.height, pre.oceanFactor, rf, rFlow);
```
(The `blendedHeight` wrapper at line 40493-40496 calls `applyRiverCarve(gx, gz, seed, pre.height, pre.oceanFactor)` with no `riverFactor`/`riverFlow` — both stay `undefined`, so `applyRiverCarve` computes `r` via its own `riverFactorAt` and reads the fresh scratch. No change needed to `blendedHeight` beyond the two trailing params defaulting to `undefined`.)

`getDeltaFingerFactor` (grep anchor `function getDeltaFingerFactor`, currently lines 42408-42429; signature today is `(gx, gz, seed, oceanFactor)` at line 42408) gains a `flowScale = 1` param: `fingerWidth` (currently `0.12 + deltaIntensity * 0.08`, line 42421) is multiplied by `1 + DELTA_FLOW_SCALE * Math.log2(Math.max(1, flowScale)) * 0.1` — deliberately the SAME `log2` shape `riverFactorAt`'s own `flowBoost` already uses (line 42203), so a big trunk's delta widens/multi-fingers noticeably while a small stream (`flow` near 1, `log2(1)=0`) gets `flowScale`'s multiplier at exactly 1 — i.e. **byte-identical to today whenever the caller passes no flow arg** (the trailing param defaults to `1`), which is the honest byte-identity story for every OTHER existing caller of `getDeltaFingerFactor` (there is exactly one call site today, the one being edited above, so this is a non-issue in practice — noted for completeness).

**New tunable:**
- `DELTA_FLOW_SCALE` (default 1.5, tested [0.5, 3]) — label "Delta Flow Scale": how strongly river flow accumulation widens/multi-fingers a delta at its ocean mouth. 0 = today's flow-blind behavior. TERRAIN-CHANGING.

**Honest caveat — RESOLVED by the design-3 threading (audit).** The original draft here flagged a same-frame ordering fragility: on the precompute path (`precalculateTerrainCaches` passes its own `rf` through, lines 42899/42902), the scratch is populated by the CALLER's `riverFactorAt`, not one inside `applyRiverCarve`, so nothing may call `riverFactorAt` for a DIFFERENT column in between. The design-3 threading above **removes this**: `precalculateTerrainCaches` reads `_riverFlowScratch.flow` on the line immediately after its own `riverFactorAt` (42899, before ANY other call) and threads the value into `applyRiverCarve`'s new `riverFlow` param, so the value travels as data and no cross-boundary global read remains. The only residual property of the scratch idiom is the one `_lightCtxScratch` already lives with — it's module-scope state referenced by an injected function, which is exactly why the worker MUST get an explicit `_riverFlowScratch` injection line (see Worker-parity disposition). The `getDeltaFingerFactor` byte-identity story is unchanged: its trailing `flowScale` param defaults to `1`, and `Math.log2(1) = 0`, so any caller passing no flow (there is exactly one call site) gets the width multiplier at exactly 1.

## Worker-parity disposition

All touched functions — `computePreRiverHeight`, `applyRiverCarve`, `getOceanFactor` (unedited but read), `getDeltaFingerFactor`, `riverFactorAt`, `reliefParam`, `isTreeSoilSurface`, and `generateTerrainPass` (the beach-gate site) — are confirmed present in the `terrainFuncs` injection array (grep anchor `const terrainFuncs = [`, currently line 20247; the array spans lines 20247-20287 and includes `computePreRiverHeight`, `applyRiverCarve`, `getRiverFactor`, `getOceanFactor`, `getRiverDepth`, `getOceanDepth`, `getDeltaFingerFactor`, `riverFactorAt`, `reliefParam`, and `precalculateTerrainCaches` by name — all re-verified present at the post-P10 build). **All WS8 edits are main-thread-source-only** — `Function.toString()` injection propagates every edit to the worker automatically; no hand-maintained worker copy exists for any of these (confirmed, none of them appear in the Lockstep Registry's byte-level-copies table). `isTreeSoilSurface` and `generateTerrainPass` are NOT in `terrainFuncs` (they're part of the main-thread mesh/material path, not the worker height path) — their mirrored-logic relationship is review-enforced per the existing Lockstep Registry entry, not injection-enforced; WS8 does not change that.

**New requirement, not optional — AUDIT-SHARPENED (right worker matters):** `_riverFlowScratch` (F3) is a module-scope object literal, exactly like `_lightCtxScratch`; the injected function SOURCE does not carry the module-scope object with it, so `buildChunkWorkerCode` MUST emit an explicit data line for it or the worker's `riverFactorAt` throws a ReferenceError on the first `_riverFlowScratch.flow` write. **Emit it into the TERRAIN/GEN worker's `injectedCode` block (grep `injectedCode +=`, the module-scope-const emission run starting ~line 20291 — e.g. `injectedCode += '    const _riverFlowScratch = { flow: 1 };\\n';` alongside the other `const X = ...` emissions), NOT into the mesh worker's `meshCode`.** This is the trap: the only existing `_lightCtxScratch` emission lives in `meshCode` (line 20583), because `_lightCtxScratch` is used by the LIGHTING/MESH worker — but `riverFactorAt` runs in the TERRAIN/GEN worker (it's in `terrainFuncs`, injected via `injectedCode`), a DIFFERENT worker. Following "same pattern as `_lightCtxScratch`'s emission" literally would put the line in the wrong worker and still ReferenceError. `parity-check.mjs` catches NEITHER the missing line NOR the wrong-worker placement (it checks the byte-level-copies table + injection markers, not "does every module-scope scratch referenced by an injected function have its own injection line in the correct worker") — verify by hand in P1 by generating a world with `hydroRivers` on and confirming no worker console ReferenceError, and consider a future parity-check enhancement.

## Harness plan

**Existing metrics to re-gate (no new code, just re-run against WS8's output):**
- **M6** (land/ocean split) — F2's relief-driven transition must not shift the ratio outside its established gate; a pure profile-SHAPE change should leave total ocean/land area untouched, but MEASURE, don't assume (the `bank` formula's average value over the transition band changes with `sharpness`, which could subtly shift where `finalHeight` crosses the render/collision floor at the margins).
- **M8** (river flood integrity) — F1's fjord bed extension changes `riverBed`/`riverDepth` at coastal crossings; verify flooded-channel-core coverage is unaffected (a mis-tuned `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE` could theoretically produce a bed so deep the existing water-fill pass behaves unexpectedly — unlikely given `fillWaterPass` floods any sub-sea-level column, but the gate is cheap to re-run and catches a sign error immediately).
- **M10/M20** (sand-water-proximity / desert dressing coherence) — F2's `isCoastCliffCol` exception removes sand from an oceanFactor arm that M10 currently expects to always produce sand; M10's tolerance band must be re-verified with cliff coasts in the mix (it should IMPROVE the "sand only near water, and only where it makes sense" story, not regress it — cliffs correctly having NO sand is the intended outcome, but M10's pass/fail arithmetic needs to agree).
- **M14** (river→ocean connectivity, hydro mode) — F1 modifies bed depth only at the coastal end of already-connected channels; springs' ocean-reachability itself (the routing/flood-spill math) is completely untouched by WS8, so M14 should be exactly unchanged — a regression here would indicate an edit leaked into routing math it shouldn't have touched.

**New metrics to add to `biome-pipeline-checks.mjs`:**
- **M22 — fjord-flooding coherence.** For sampled river-crosses-coast sites (channel core within `FJORD_COAST_BAND` of sea level AND `oceanFactor` transitioning through the coastal band along the channel's own course), verify the extended bed actually FLOODS (post-carve height at/below sea level) for at least some qualifying threshold (e.g. ≥80% of sampled high-relief crossings, gate value to be calibrated in P0 — the assignment's own phrasing "channel cores crossing coast are wet ≥ X%" leaves X for calibration, not invention here). Low-relief crossings are explicitly NOT required to flood further than today's delta/estuary behavior — the metric must separate the two populations by `reliefParam`, mirroring how M4 already excludes river-influenced pairs from its own ratio population.
- **M23 — cliff-profile presence.** Sample paired transects across the coastline at matched `oceanFactor` gradients, one set at high-`R` columns and one at low-`R` columns (same method family as the existing hillshade/transect tooling in `tools/terrain-probe.mjs`); measure the horizontal distance over which `finalHeight` crosses from "clearly land" to "clearly seafloor" (e.g. the 10%-90% band of the full land-to-floor height delta). Gate: high-R transition width measurably narrower than low-R transition width (a ratio threshold, calibrated in P0 — the assignment's framing "high-R coast transition width < low-R transition width" is a direction, the actual ratio needs real-data calibration exactly like M18-S's thresholds were calibrated against a known-terraced flank before being frozen).

## Editor implications

New `GEN_TUNABLES` keys under the `'Coastal Erosion'` schema section appear automatically in BOTH the create-world Advanced Tunables UI (`populateGenParamControls`) and the terrain-parameter-editor's Advanced Tunables panel (`groupSchemaBySections`) — no bespoke UI code needed, same mechanism every prior workstream's tunables used. Hover-tooltip notes follow the WS6-P9 convention exactly (see that as-built): the `note` field renders as a `title` attribute on the row's label (both builders), NOT always-visible inline text; labels do NOT redundantly restate the section name (no "Coastal Fjord Reach" — just "Fjord Coast Band (blk)", mirroring how WS6-P9 stripped the redundant "Hydro" prefix from every river row now that the section itself is titled "Rivers"); notes do NOT use the retired "Inert unless X is on" phrasing (these tunables are live at their defaults' non-zero values from day one, so there is no "inert" state to describe — unlike WS1's `TERRACE_WARP_AMP=0`, WS8 has no all-zero-is-off design; see Version impact below for why). Live-dialing: `computePreRiverHeight`/`applyRiverCarve`/`getDeltaFingerFactor` are all UNCACHED per-column pure functions (no `biomeCellCache`/`treeMaskCache`-style memoization sits between a tunable edit and the next render), so dialing any WS8 tunable in the editor or create-world preview takes effect on the VERY NEXT `blendedHeight`/preview-tile call, no cache-clear plumbing needed. **AUDIT — the FJORD/`hydroRegionCache` interaction specifically (reasoned through):** WS6-P6 had a cache-clear bug because a river tunable fed `buildHydroRegion`, whose output IS memoized in `hydroRegionCache`. WS8 is SAFE from that class of bug because **no WS8 tunable feeds `buildHydroRegion`** — F1's fjord deepening rides `riverFactorAt`'s OUTPUT and the `applyRiverCarve` arithmetic (both uncached), NOT the traced region geometry; F2/F3 don't touch rivers' routing at all. So `FJORD_*`/`CLIFF_*`/`DELTA_FLOW_SCALE` edits never need a `hydroRegionCache` clear — the "no cache-clear needed" claim holds precisely because WS8 leaves the one memoized river structure untouched. `climCache` (read by `isCoastCliffCol`) is rebuilt fresh per chunk-generation inside `precalculateTerrainCaches`, never persisted across edits, so it's effectively uncached w.r.t. tunable dialing too. (F3's `_riverFlowScratch` is overwritten every `riverFactorAt` call, never stale.)

## Phased plan

- **P0 — Node prototype + metric baselines.** Before touching `voxEx.html`: implement F1/F2/F3's formulas standalone in `tools/scratch/` against the REAL extracted functions (`tools/lib/extract-terrain.mjs`'s `buildTerrainApi`, same method every prior WS used), render hillshades at real coastlines (mountainous AND flat, per the honesty standard WS6-P7's as-built set — "measurably wider at close zoom, imperceptible at map scale" is an acceptable and expected kind of finding here too, report it if true rather than overclaiming). Calibrate M22/M23's numeric gates against known fjord-candidate and known-flat coastlines the way M18-S was calibrated against the known-terraced flank. Resolve F1's honest risk (fjord/delta collision at the same crossing) here — either prove the relief gate separates them cleanly or add the explicit mutual-exclusion the risk note flags.
- **P1 — implement behind the new tunables; be honest about what CAN and CANNOT be neutral-staged.** Unlike WS1's `TERRACE_WARP_AMP=0` (a true off-switch reducing to a no-op comparison) or WS4's frequency retune (byte-identical at CURRENT frequency values), **F1 and F2 have no all-zero neutral state that reduces to today's exact formula bit-for-bit AT A NON-DEFAULT-LOOKING VALUE** — F1's fjord depth term is additive and IS zero when `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE=0` (a genuine neutral default is possible: ship P1 with `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE` defaulted to 0, byte-identical, and only set it non-zero when Bump-[next] flips), and F2's `sharpness` formula reduces exactly to the current cubic when `sharpness===1`. **AUDIT FIX — the original draft proposed `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN=1.0` as F2's neutral-staging default; that is BROKEN.** With `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN=1.0` the denominator `(1 - CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN)` is 0: for `rC < 1` the divide gives `-Infinity` (harmlessly clamped to `cliffT=0`), but for `rC === 1.0` EXACTLY it is `0/0 = NaN`, which propagates `sharpness→NaN→bank→NaN→finalHeight→NaN`. Whether any column hits `rC === 1.0` depends on `SPLINE_RELIEF`'s cap (splines often plateau at 1.0), so this is NOT provably byte-identical — it's a latent NaN-terrain bug. **Use `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX=1` as F2's neutral-staging default instead** (with `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN` at its real 0.55): then `sharpness = 1 + cliffT*(1-1) = 1` for ALL `cliffT`, so `bank` is byte-identical to today's cubic regardless of `rC`, AND `cliffDen = Math.max(1e-6, 0.45)` never divides by zero. (The `cliffDen` guard added to the After sketch also makes any user-dialed `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN >= 1.0` safe at runtime, not just at the staging default.) F1's fjord depth term is genuinely neutral at `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE=0` (additive `riverDepth += 0` with `FJORD_RELIEF_MIN` at its real 0.5 so its own denominator is 0.5, not 0 — do NOT stage F1 by zeroing `FJORD_RELIEF_MIN`, same div-by-zero trap). F3 is naturally staged (`DELTA_FLOW_SCALE=0` is an exact no-op `1 + 0` multiplier). **So: P1 CAN ship fully byte-identical** with landing defaults `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE=0`, `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX=1` (NOT `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN=1.0`), `DELTA_FLOW_SCALE=0` — prove it by the same direct-diff method every prior workstream used (2401+ cols × 3 seeds vs a pre-edit snapshot, checking for NaN explicitly) before claiming it, don't assume it from the formulas alone.
- **P2 — flip with ONE `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` bump.** Retune `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE`/`CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN`/`DELTA_FLOW_SCALE` to their real (non-neutral) shipped defaults in a single batched commit, same "additive plumbing lands anytime, VALUE change flips together" discipline as Bump A. In-game eyeball last (real fjord-candidate coastlines — river mouths in mountainous biomes — and a real cliff coastline, per the mandatory last rung of the verification ladder).

## Risks

- **M6 shift** — any land/ocean area change from F2's transition-shape edit must be caught by re-running M6, not assumed zero from "it's just a shape change" reasoning (see M6 re-gate above).
- **Beach-gate interplay** — `isCoastCliffCol`'s exception is one more OR-branch in an already-dense material cascade (swamp, desert, tundra, alpine all layer onto the same depth-0 surface-block ladder); P0/P1 must re-run the FULL cascade's existing gates (M9/M10/M11/M20), not just the new cliff case in isolation, exactly as WS5 had to when it added desert/tundra branches to the same cascade.
- **Fjord/delta collision at the same mouth** — called out in detail under F1; unresolved pending P0.
- **Cost: R sampling per coastal column** — F2's `reliefParam()` call inside `computePreRiverHeight` is a genuine new fbm cost on a function that already runs once per column in the main generation loop (not reusable from `climCache`, which is built in a later pass of the same enclosing function — see F2's AUDIT NOTE). This must be measured against the ~1.10µs `getPreRiverHeight`-dominates-everything baseline WS6 recorded, the same way WS2 measured its own `classifyBiome`-inside-`terrainSurface` redundancy at 2.28× before deciding whether/how to amortize. If the cost gate fails, the likely fix is the SAME shape WS2 used for T/H (a quantized-coordinate module-scope memo for R specifically inside the ocean-blend path) — not invented here, flagged as the fallback lever if P0's measurement demands it.
- **Worker-parity miss on `_riverFlowScratch`** — see the explicit callout under Worker-parity disposition; this is a real, specific way to ship a worker ReferenceError that `parity-check.mjs` will NOT catch today.

## Version impact

One `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` bump at the P2 flip (`FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE`/`CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX`/`DELTA_FLOW_SCALE` moving off their P1 neutral-staging values — note the staging-flip tunable is `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX`, per the P1 audit fix, not `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN`). WS6-P10 (stream-capture) has now LANDED at build `2026-07-14.2`, TGV 41 — WS8's own flip lands on whatever bump number is next-available at implementation time; do not hardcode a number here, re-check `const TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` at hand-off. `CURRENT_CACHE_VERSION`/`SETTINGS_VERSION`: no (no material/light-adjacency semantics change; the beach-gate exception is a material SELECTION change, not an attenuation/lighting change).

**WS8 draft AUDITED (opus, 2026-07-14).** Method: File-tool Grep/Read against the live post-WS6-P10 `voxEx.html` (build `2026-07-14.2`, TGV 41, ~52.8K lines); bash used only to run gates from `/sessions/.../voxex/`. Every WS8 Before snippet re-verified VERBATIM-correct; all edit-site functions confirmed present and in `terrainFuncs`. Verdict: **WS8 is ready for P0 implementation** with the corrections below applied to the draft.

**Corrections made to the draft (this pass):**
1. **Line-hint refresh** (WS6-P10 drifted everything after ~41800 by +77, a few earlier lines by +10). Old→new: computePreRiverHeight ocean block 40369-76 → **40379-86** (fn 40351); applyRiverCarve inRiver block 40439-70 → **40449-80**, riverDepth/riverBed → **40459-60**, fn header → **40401**, delta call 40404 → **40411-14**; beach gate 43176-90 → **43253-67**; `_colBiomeId` ~43130-33 → **43207-10**; `isCliff` 43090 → **43145**; `isTreeSoilSurface` 5950-64 → **5951-65**; riverFactorAt `bestFlow` 42177 → **42254**, `flowBoost` 42203 → **42280**, `haveElevation` ~42115-23 → **42188-42201**, bucket search 42162-200 → **42255-77**; getDeltaFingerFactor 42331-52 → **42408-29**, fingerWidth 42344 → **42421**; precalc main loop ~42832 → **42891-42910** (computePreRiverHeight 42896 / riverFactorAt 42899 / applyRiverCarve 42902), climCache pass ~42862 → **42926+**; `_lightCtxScratch` ~42495 → **42572** (main decl), worker emission at **20583** (meshCode); terrainFuncs array 20246 → **20247** (spans 20247-87). climCache confirmed 4 plane-major planes T/H/Cn/**R (plane 3)**, planeStride = chunkSize² (line 42927-28).
2. **F2 div-by-zero (SEVERITY: HIGH, would ship NaN terrain).** The proposed P1 neutral-staging default `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN=1.0` makes `(1 - CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN)=0`; `rC===1.0` exactly → `0/0=NaN` → NaN height. Fixed: staging default is now `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX=1` (byte-identical for all `cliffT`, no zero denominator); added a runtime `cliffDen = Math.max(1e-6, 1-CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN)` guard to the F2 After sketch (protects user-dialed `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN>=1`); hoisted the double `Math.pow` to a single `ofP`. Same trap flagged for F1 (`FJORD_RELIEF_MIN` must NOT be staged to 1.0 — stage via `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE=0` only).
3. **F2 material-gate defects (SEVERITY: MED, would crash on legacy path).** `isCoastCliffCol` sketch read `caches.climCache[...]` unguarded (TypeError when `biomeDrivenTerrain=false`, climCache null) and used `planeStride`, which is block-local to the climCache builder and NOT in scope at the beach gate. Rewrote the sketch with a `(worldConfig.biomeDrivenTerrain && caches.climCache) ? ... : false` guard mirroring `_colBiomeId`, and `3 * chunkSize * chunkSize` inline instead of `planeStride`.
4. **F3 design call (see below) — rewrote "Exposing bestFlow", the consumer edit sketch, and the honest caveat.**
5. **F3 return enumeration.** Draft said "1 on both early-outs"; audit found `riverFactorAt` has **FIVE** returns (42185 ribbon, 42278 no-poly, 42299 tiny-halfWidth, 42301 outside-channel, 42303 in-channel). Enumerated all five with the value each must write.
6. **Worker-parity sharpened.** `_riverFlowScratch` must be emitted into the TERRAIN worker's `injectedCode` (~20291), NOT the mesh worker's `meshCode` where the existing `_lightCtxScratch` line (20583) lives — riverFactorAt runs in a different worker. Following the draft's original "same as `_lightCtxScratch`" pointer literally would ReferenceError.
7. **F1 cStr interplay** confirmed sound and documented (fjord band `preHeight<75` sits below the cStr fade 82-95, so full carve applies) — accounts for WS6-P7's "cStr zeroes carving above 95" fact.
8. **F2 cost-reuse levers** added (restrict to `oceanFactor<1` columns first; optionally reorder cornerR grid before the main loop for free R; memo as last resort). **Editor paragraph** corrected to explain WHY no `hydroRegionCache` clear is needed (no WS8 tunable feeds `buildHydroRegion`). **Fjord/delta collision** given a concrete P0 exit-gate experiment with a decision rule and mutual-exclusion fallback.

**The F3 design call (auditor's decision).** Rejected the draft's design-2 (module-scope scratch read ACROSS the caller boundary) for its latent same-frame ordering fragility, and rejected design-1 (sibling `riverFlowAt`) on the per-column re-query cost WS6-P7 already ruled out. **Chose design-3:** keep `_riverFlowScratch` as the unavoidable zero-alloc transport OUT of `riverFactorAt` (writing it on all five returns; worker injection line required), but thread the flow value as an explicit `riverFlow` parameter on `applyRiverCarve` (mirroring the existing `riverFactor` param) so every scratch READ sits one line below its WRITE and the value travels as data across boundaries — removing the fragility by construction at the cost of one parameter. Verified the current call graph makes this clean (precalc 42899→42902 same column; blendedHeight path uses applyRiverCarve's own internal riverFactorAt).

**Residual risks flagged in the draft (for P0/P1, not blockers):**
- **Mirror R-source divergence** — cascade reads coarse bilinear climCache R, `isTreeSoilSurface` reads exact `reliefParam`; can disagree at the `CLIFF_RELIEF_MIN` threshold on boundary columns. Low practical impact (elevation ladder rejects high-relief columns as tree soil anyway); documented with a "use one R source or accept it" decision for P0.
- **F2 R-sampling cost** — genuine new uncached fbm per ocean-blend column; must be measured against the ~1.10µs baseline at P0 before flip (levers pre-staged).
- **Fjord/delta collision** — unresolved by design; P0 exit-gate experiment now specified.

**Overall: nothing blocks the WS8 → P0 hand-off.** All corrections are in the draft; the two would-ship-broken items (F2 div-by-zero staging, F2 material-gate null/scope) are fixed in the sketches so a P1 implementer copying them lands safe code. M22/M23 are implementable in the existing biome-metrics patterns (M4-style population separation, M18-S-style calibration, terrain-probe transects). WS6-P10 was independently audited the same pass and passed all gates (see its own As-built above; gate re-run confirmed: syntax/parity green, biome-pipeline `--hydro` seed 1337 all-green incl. M14/M15/M16/M17, terrain-node-checks green, browser suite 405/405).
