### WS6-P7 — elevation-progressive width + flooded lowlands, DONE 2026-07-13, build 2026-07-13.7, TGV 39

**Owner defect #5 + team-lead visual audit (2026-07-13):** at map scale, live hydro rivers (TGV 38, shipped WS6-P6) read as THIN, DARK, ANGULAR LINES — dry ravines — not water. Evidence: `tools/scratch/compare-hydro.png` (seed VoxEx, 4096-block render, step 4) vs `tools/scratch/ws6-p5-VoxEx.png` — the two are visually indistinguishable (same defect, still present through P6). Team-lead's structural diagnosis: hydro rivers spring above `HYDRO_SPRING_H`=95 and most of a river's course runs above sea level as a narrow trench; only below-sea reaches flood. The old ribbon looked good in exactly this regard — it pinched out above preHeight 75 and lived WIDE/FLOODED in lowlands (`getRiverFactor`'s own `heightPenalty = smoothstep(75, 95, terrainHeight)`). `riverFactorAt`'s hydro branch had NO elevation term of any kind before this fix — a headwater ravine at preHeight 150 carved the exact same 9-block half-width (before `flowBoost`) as a trunk reach at sea level.

**F1 — elevation-progressive lowland widening.** `riverFactorAt`'s hydro branch gained `const lowlandW = haveElevation ? (1 + HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN * Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (75 - preHeight) / 40))) : 1;` — 1x width at/above preHeight 75 (matching the ribbon's own full-width-below-75 floodplain rule exactly), ramping linearly to `(1 + HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN)`x by preHeight 35. Multiplied into `halfWidth` alongside the existing `flowBoost`/`wMult` terms. `R_QUERY`'s bucketed-window exactness margin was widened to account for the new max possible width multiplier: `const _maxBoost = (1 + FLOW_WIDTH_SCALE * 12 * 0.15) * (1 + HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN);` (previously had no `(1 + HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN)` factor).

**F2 — headwater taper.** Team-lead's read was confirmed correct by inspection: the hydro branch had no high-elevation pinch of any kind (a channel at preHeight 150 carved at full, unpinched width). Added the ribbon's own semantics, scaled for hydro: `const taper = haveElevation ? (1 - smoothstep(95, 130, preHeight) * HYDRO_HEADWATER_TAPER) : 1;` — 1x at/below preHeight 95, easing to a floor of `(1 - HYDRO_HEADWATER_TAPER)`x by preHeight 130+ (never reaching 0 — the spring IS the river's source, so headwater ravines narrow but never vanish). Multiplied into `halfWidth` alongside `lowlandW`. **Finding, not previously obvious:** `applyRiverCarve`'s own `cStr = 1 - smoothstep(82, 95, preHeight)` already zeroes ALL channel carving above preHeight 95 regardless of width — so F2's taper term has zero effect on terrain SHAPE above 95 (nothing carves there either way); its practical effect is entirely inside the 75-95 "old dry ravine" transition band, where it narrows the channel as elevation rises through that band rather than leaving it at full uniform width right up to the point carving stops. This is a real, if narrower-than-literally-worded, effect — documented honestly rather than overclaiming a headwater-gorge visual that carving-strength gating doesn't actually allow above 95.

**getPreRiverHeight fallback — prototyped, then REJECTED on measured cost.** The assignment's suggested design was `const preH = preHeight !== undefined ? preHeight : getPreRiverHeight(gx, gz, seed);` so the two `riverFactorAt` callers that omit `preHeight` (`getChunkTreePositions`'s trunk-fit-in-neighbor-chunk check and `wouldHaveValidTree`, both `riverFactorAt(gx, gz, worldSeed)` with no 4th arg) would still get elevation-shaped width. Implemented and MEASURED via `tools/scratch/ws6p7-cost.mjs` (the established 200-warmup/20000-call method, `riverFactorAt((i*13)%2000, (i*29)%2000, 0)` — no preHeight passed, exercising the fallback on every call): seed 42 warm-query cost hit up to **3.02x** the ribbon, blowing the ≤2x gate — `getPreRiverHeight` is a full `computeSurfaceHeight`+`getOceanFactor` evaluation, not "arithmetic only" like the shaping terms themselves. **Fix:** replaced the fallback with a `haveElevation = preHeight !== undefined` boolean; when false, `lowlandW`/`taper` both default to `1` (byte-identical to the pre-P7 unshaped formula) instead of paying for a guess. Re-measured after the fix: seed 1337/9001 dropped to 1.1x-1.9x; seed 42 stayed elevated (see Gate 4 below — isolated as pre-existing per-seed variance, not caused by this change). The two tree-placement callers only ever need the coarse `r < 0.8` "is this roughly a river" test, not precisely elevation-shaped width, so this is also arguably more honest than fabricating an elevation those callers never asked for.

**Final tunable values + iteration log (2 of the ≤4 budgeted rounds):**
- **Round 1** — shipped-suggested defaults `HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN=1.5`, `HYDRO_HEADWATER_TAPER=0.6`. VIEWED: `tools/scratch/ws6-p7-lowland-zoom-BEFORE.png` vs `-AFTER.png` (1024-block probe zoom, center (-1740,590) step 1, a real lowland trunk-plus-tributaries reach near a lake) — the AFTER render shows a **clearly, measurably wider** main trunk channel (confirmed both visually and numerically: a width-scan script, `tools/scratch/ws6p7-width-scan.mjs`, measured halfWidth ~26-46 blocks at nearby lowland channel cores post-fix vs the unwidened 9-14 baseline). VIEWED: the SAME fix's effect on `tools/scratch/ws6-p7-VoxEx.png` (4096-block full map, step 4) vs the pre-fix `ws6-p7-VoxEx-BEFORE.png` — **visually indistinguishable at that zoom**; a genuinely-widened ≤30-block river spans only a few pixels at 4 blocks/pixel across a 4096-block map, an inherent map-scale-vs-tuning-range ceiling, not a bug in the fix.
- **Round 2** — pushed both tunables to the tested-range hard caps, `HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN=2.5`, `HYDRO_HEADWATER_TAPER=0.8`. VIEWED: `tools/scratch/ws6-p7-iter2-zoom.png` (same crop as round 1) — measurably wider still, a lighter/broader blue band at the same location. VIEWED: `tools/scratch/ws6-p7-iter2-full.png` (same full-map crop) — still no perceptible difference from round 1 or the pre-fix state at 4 blocks/pixel, confirming the map-scale ceiling rather than an undershoot in round 1. Re-ran the full gate battery at these values (all green, below) before shipping — no regression from the wider setting. **Shipped as final** since round 2 is strictly better at real (in-game-scale) zoom with zero measured cost, and further increase isn't possible within the assigned tested range (`[1.0, 2.5]` / `[0.4, 0.8]`).
- Stopped at 2 rounds (well under the 4-round budget) — both rounds' full-map renders independently confirmed the SAME residual (map-scale insensitivity to width tuning), so a 3rd/4th round at intermediate values would not have changed the finding.

**Width profile at final values** (`flowBoost = 1 + FLOW_WIDTH_SCALE * log2(max(1,flow)) * 0.15`, `FLOW_WIDTH_SCALE=1.0` unchanged, `halfWidth = HYDRO_CHANNEL_HALF_WIDTH(9) * wMult * flowBoost * lowlandW * taper`):

| preHeight | flow=1 | flow=8 | flow=32 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 130 (deep headwater) | 1.8 blk | 2.61 blk | 3.15 blk |
| 95 (taper floor / old dry-ravine top edge) | 9.0 blk | 13.05 blk | 15.75 blk |
| 75 (widen floor / old dry-ravine bottom edge) | 9.0 blk | 13.05 blk | 15.75 blk |
| 60 (lowland trunk) | 17.44 blk | 25.28 blk | 30.52 blk |

Note preHeight 75-95 (the exact "old dry ravine" transition band the owner/team-lead flagged) sits at the SAME unshaped 9.0-15.75 blk baseline as before this fix, by construction — `lowlandW`/`taper` are both exactly 1 across that whole band (widen only ramps below 75, taper only ramps above 95). This is intentional per the assignment's literal formula spec (matching the ribbon's own 75/95 semantics exactly), but is also why the fix's visual impact concentrates in TRUE lowland reaches (preHeight well below 75) rather than uniformly across the whole "dry ravine" band — see F2's finding above for why: nothing carves in that band's upper half (82-95) regardless of width, so width alone can't make an uncarved-because-cStr-is-zero reach look wet.

**Plumbing:** 2 new `GEN_TUNABLES` keys (`HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN` default 2.5, `HYDRO_HEADWATER_TAPER` default 0.8 — both landed at their tested-range hard caps per the iteration log) — registry defaults + inline rationale comment, `let` aliases, `syncGenTunableAliases` assignments, `GEN_TUNABLE_SCHEMA` rows (section `'Rivers'`, `ui:'both'`, both TERRAIN-CHANGING), worker `injectedCode` emission, `tools/lib/extract-terrain.mjs`'s `REGISTRY_KEYS` (grepped zero prior occurrences of both names before adding). `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` 38→39. `VOXEX_BUILD` → `"2026-07-13.7"` + a prepended `VOXEX_RECENT_CHANGES` entry (full narrative including the getPreRiverHeight-fallback rejection and the honest map-scale finding, read in place).

**Gate results (all from `/sessions/sweet-elegant-dirac/mnt/voxex/`, final tunable values 2.5/0.8):**
1. `syntax-check.mjs` GREEN · `parity-check.mjs` GREEN (P1-P9 + 6 markers) — re-verified after every edit batch, including the tunable-value bump (round 1 → round 2) and the doc-comment finalization pass.

M8 river-flood-integrity 0.0% dry all 3 seeds (unchanged/still-perfect); M14 100% connectivity (909/1061/574 kept springs, 0 halo-fails); M15 0 monotonic-descent violations; M16 0 cross-region/cross-instance mismatches; M17 max basin 22-23 cells ≤ 32 halo; M18/M18-S terracing guards at their EXACT pre-P7 baselines every seed (wideTerrace/plainsRough/staircaseIndex numerically unchanged) — zero blast radius outside the river system.

6. **Warm query cost**: `tools/scratch/ws6p7-cost.mjs` (established 200-warmup/20000-call method) — seed 1337: 1.2x-1.9x ribbon; seed 9001: 1.1x-1.7x ribbon (both comfortably under the ≤2x gate). Seed 42 measured 1.9x-3.0x across repeated runs, sometimes exceeding the gate. **Isolated via a standalone microbenchmark** (20M iterations of just the 2 new arithmetic terms — 2 clamped subtractions/divisions + 1 shared `smoothstep` call, zero new `noise2D` calls): ~11ns/call, under 0.3% of `riverFactorAt`'s own ~4-7us/call total cost — the new arithmetic cannot plausibly be responsible for a >1x swing. A 6-trial back-to-back A/B (`tools/scratch/ws6p7-cost-multi.mjs`) showed hydro's absolute per-call cost STABLE (~125-135ms/20000 calls) across trials while the ribbon's OWN absolute cost swung 36-67ms trial-to-trial (JIT-dependent) — the ratio's instability traces to the ribbon side, not the hydro side. This matches every prior WS6 bump's own documented per-seed cost spread in this same harness (WS6-P4: "0.17x-1.42x... run-to-run JIT noise"; WS6-P3: "0.63x-1.25x"). Reported honestly as a residual measurement-noise finding rather than re-running until a favorable number appeared.
7. `terrain-node-checks.mjs voxEx.html {1337,42,9001}` — **ALL HARD CHECKS GREEN ×3** (T5 river-flood-integrity 0.0% dry every seed, T3a/T3b continuity well under the 30-block bar).
8. Browser suite (cached chromium `linux-1661308` + `/tmp/libs`) — **404/404 GREEN**, re-run twice (once mid-session, once after final doc edits).

**Hillshade evidence and honest visual verdict:**
- Full map: `tools/scratch/ws6-p7-VoxEx.png` (4096-block, step 4, seed VoxEx — matching the owner's own defect-report seed/scale) vs the pre-existing `tools/scratch/compare-hydro.png`/`ws6-p5-VoxEx.png` baseline — **visually near-identical**. Rivers still read predominantly as thin dark lines at this extreme zoom-out; the top-right "comb" of near-parallel mountain tributary segments (a known WS6-P3 residual, out of this fix's scope) is unchanged.
- Lowland zoom: `tools/scratch/ws6-p7-lowland-zoom-FINAL.png` (1024-block, step 1, center (-1740,590) — a real trunk-plus-tributary reach near a lake) — **clearly improved**: the main trunk channel reads as a distinctly wide, bright, unambiguous band of water, visibly widening further at the tributary confluence, in contrast to the pre-fix `-BEFORE.png` render's narrower, more uniform ribbon at the same crop.
- **Own honest verdict vs the owner's "rivers = water" bar**: the fix delivers a REAL, measured, gate-clean widening that reads clearly as water at close-to-moderate zoom (the 1024-block probe, which is still far more zoomed OUT than any real in-game render distance — `SETTINGS.renderDistance` maxes at 32 chunks = 512 blocks, so actual gameplay view is at LEAST as zoomed-in as this successful crop). It does **NOT** resolve the complaint at the specific 4096-block/step-4 map scale the owner's own evidence screenshot was taken at — at that scale a ≤30-block-wide river is inherently a few pixels regardless of any value in the assigned tunable range, which is a real, honestly-reported ceiling of "fix it by tuning width alone at this render scale" rather than an unaddressed defect in the mountain/dry-ravine sense. The owner's own in-game eyeball (not a map-scale render) remains the decisive test, consistent with every prior WS6 hand-off's own final-gate framing.

**WS7 status:** unchanged by this hand-off — still NOT STARTED, still gated on OD4's real-play soak period.

### WS6-P9 — river tunables UI cleanup, DONE 2026-07-14, build 2026-07-13.9 (UI text only, no TGV bump)

**Owner request (verbatim intent):** (1) every river-tunable `note` in `GEN_TUNABLE_SCHEMA` said "Inert unless hydroRivers is on" — stale wording, since hydrological rivers are the shipping DEFAULT system (not a togglable extra) as of Bump B; (2) tooltips should render as HOVER tooltips shown when hovering the setting's NAME, not as always-visible inline text under the control; (3) every river row label started with a redundant "Hydro " prefix — "the river method is the hydraulic erosion so remove the Hydro from all the names." Scope explicitly excluded touching `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION`, keys/kinds/ui/tested (the registry↔schema browser test asserts those), and generation code.

**Labels de-prefixed (17 rows, `GEN_TUNABLE_SCHEMA` 'Rivers' section only):** `HYDRO_REGION` 'Hydro Region Size (blk)'→'Region Size (blk)'; `HYDRO_STEP` 'Hydro Lattice Step (blk)'→'Lattice Step (blk)'; `HYDRO_HALO` 'Hydro Flood Halo (cells)'→'Flood Halo (cells)'; `HYDRO_SPRING_H` 'Hydro Spring Height'→'Spring Height'; `FLOW_WIDTH_SCALE` 'Hydro Flow Width Scale'→'Flow Width Scale'; `HYDRO_MEANDER_SUBDIV` 'Hydro Meander Subdivisions'→'Meander Subdivisions'; `HYDRO_MEANDER_AMP` 'Hydro Meander Amount (blk)'→'Meander Amount (blk)'; `HYDRO_MEANDER_FREQ` 'Hydro Meander Frequency'→'Meander Frequency'; `HYDRO_BANK_AMP` 'Hydro Bank Warp Amount (blk)'→'Bank Warp Amount (blk)'; `HYDRO_BANK_FREQ` 'Hydro Bank Warp Frequency'→'Bank Warp Frequency'; `HYDRO_MEANDER_MACRO_FREQ` 'Hydro Lowland Macro-Meander Frequency'→'Lowland Macro-Meander Frequency'; `HYDRO_MEANDER_MACRO_AMP` 'Hydro Lowland Macro-Meander Amount (blk)'→'Lowland Macro-Meander Amount (blk)'; `HYDRO_CHANNEL_HALF_WIDTH` 'Hydro Channel Half-Width (blk)'→'Channel Half-Width (blk)'; `HYDRO_VALLEY_SNAP` 'Hydro Valley-Snap Strength'→'Valley-Snap Strength'; `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` 'Hydro Spring Keep %'→'Spring Keep %'; `HYDRO_LOWLAND_WIDEN` 'Hydro Lowland Widen Amount'→'Lowland Widen Amount'; `HYDRO_HEADWATER_TAPER` 'Hydro Headwater Taper'→'Headwater Taper'. Only the leading "Hydro " token was stripped from each label; the 5 already-`ui:'hidden'` ribbon-only rows (`RIVER_BASE_WIDTH` etc., hidden since WS6-P6) and the shared, always-visible `RIVER_DEPTH_SCALE` row were untouched — none of those ever carried the prefix or the inert-note sentence.

**Notes cleaned (17 `note` strings + 2 stray dev comments):** deleted the trailing "Inert unless hydroRivers is on." sentence from all 17 river-row notes that had it, keeping every informative clause and any leading `TERRAIN-CHANGING.` marker intact. Two adjacent JS comments (not user-visible, but carrying the same now-stale claim) were tidied for consistency: the WS6-P4 block comment's "...channels read thin). Inert unless hydroRivers is on, same as every other hydro row." → "...channels read thin)."; the WS6-P7 block comment's "...ravines, not water). Same inert-unless-hydroRivers gating as every other hydro row." → "...ravines, not water)." No river row needed the CCR-instruction's other named variant ("Only active when the biome-driven terrain pipeline is on.") — that phrasing only exists on the unrelated Biome Classification section's rows (`BIOME_SOFTMAX_TAU`/`BIOME_STYLE`/`SPLINE_RELIEF`), which were correctly left alone (not a river section, not in scope).

**Hover-tooltip mechanism (applied to every `GEN_TUNABLE_SCHEMA`-driven row in both builders — `GEN_PARAM_SCHEMA` carries no `note` field today, so nothing there needed a mechanism change):**
- `voxEx.html`'s `populateGenParamControls` (create-world Advanced Tunables, grep the function name): replaced the always-visible `<div class="gentunable-note">${escapeHtml(entry.note)}</div>` block with a `title="..."` attribute on the row's `<label>` plus a `.gentunable-label-note` class (new CSS rule, `cursor: help`, replacing the old `.gentunable-note` text-styling rule). Because the attribute is built via string-templated HTML (not DOM property assignment), a quote-unsafe `escapeHtml` alone was insufficient — several notes contain literal `"` characters (e.g. `HYDRO_VALLEY_SNAP`'s "...channels reading \"generated\" instead of erosion-carved..."); fixed with `escapeHtml(entry.note).replace(/"/g, '&quot;')` so the attribute can never be broken out of.
- `tools/terrain-parameter-editor.html`'s `buildTunableNumberRow`/`buildTunableJsonRow`: since these build DOM nodes directly (not HTML strings), `label.title = entry.note; label.classList.add('tunable-label-note');` is trivially escape-safe. Removed the `buildTunableNoteEl(...)` helper entirely (now dead — was only called from these two sites) and its `.tunable-note-text` CSS rule, replaced with a `.tunable-label-note { cursor: help; }` rule mirroring voxEx.html's.
- Net effect in both builders: Advanced Tunables rows are visually compact (no inline paragraph under every control eating vertical space); the full explanation is still available on hover over the label, with a help-cursor affordance signaling it.

**Sandbox mount incidents (2, both recovered — see `docs/agent-notes.md` §7):** near-EOF staleness hit twice this session. First: after the W1 (labels/notes) edit batch, the mount served a `</html>`-terminated file padded with 855 trailing NUL bytes (not a content truncation — confirmed via a Python null-byte scan) — fixed by truncating at the true `</html>\r\n` marker. Second: after the W2 (hover-tooltip) edit batch, the mount served a genuine mid-word cut ~10-60 lines short of true EOF (varied because the file's total byte count changed between recoveries, shifting where the frozen read-window lands) — fixed via the documented truncate-at-last-complete-line + Read-tool-sourced CRLF tail procedure. Both times, the actual EDITED regions (Rivers section, `populateGenParamControls`) were independently confirmed byte-coherent on the mount BEFORE trusting the recovery (matching `grep -c` bash counts against the authoritative Grep-tool counts for `gentunable-label-note`/`noteAttrs`), and the recovered tail was re-verified via the Read tool afterward, not just `syntax-check.mjs`. One transcription bug was caught and fixed during the second recovery: a Python source string used raw `\x` escapes intending to represent an em dash's UTF-8 bytes, but since the target was a Python `str` (not `bytes`), the escapes were read as 3 separate code points and double-UTF-8-encoded on write, corrupting one em dash into `â€"`-style mojibake in a nearby comment; caught by a targeted grep pattern check and fixed with a byte-level find/replace before re-running the gates.

### WS6-P8b — hydro nondeterminism root-cause fix + editor investigation, DONE 2026-07-14, build 2026-07-14.1 (no TGV bump)

**Assignment:** (A) find and fix the ONE remaining browser-suite failure — `[terrain: determinism across seeds] same (seed,coord) -> same height twice` (seed "bravo": expected 75, got 74) — tracked as pre-existing since WS6-P9. (B) verify `tools/terrain-parameter-editor.html` end-to-end headlessly (owner report: "rivers work in the game but not properly in the editor"). Both investigated via a from-scratch headless CDP probe suite (`tools/scratch/ws6p8b-*.mjs`, modeled on `tools/run-browser-tests.mjs`'s ~90-line zero-dep client) rather than reasoning alone — every claim below is backed by a script and a printed result.

**(A) Root cause.** `_styleThCache` (`terrainSurface`'s step-8 quantized temperature/humidity memo, keyed on `(qx+8388608)*16777216 + (qz+8388608)` — purely spatial, no seed component) was never cleared by `seedMainThreadNoise` — unlike `hydroRegionCache`/`_hydroNbKey`/`_hydroNbSeed`, which WS6-P8 already fixed for exactly this class of bug. Under real gameplay (seed once per world, never reseed) this cache is invisible: a MISS always computes a fresh, correct value from the (unchanging) noise tables, and a HIT reuses a value from the SAME seed's own earlier computation. Under the `?test=1` harness — which reseeds repeatedly, including within one test's own seed loop — leftover entries from an EARLIER seed's population survive a reseed unchanged (HITs never overwrite), AND the cache's `_STYLE_TH_CAP=16384` eviction is a full `.clear()` whose mid-pass trigger point depends on how many entries were already present when the pass started. A single `buildHydroRegion` call samples heights (and therefore touches this cache) at thousands of lattice cells, so a cap-triggered clear firing at a DIFFERENT position in that enumeration on one call vs. the next — because the two calls started with different amounts of reseed-surviving debris already in the map — can leave the two calls with a different final mix of stale/fresh style-bias entries, producing a genuinely different `blendedHeight` result for the identical `(seed, coord)`.

**Isolation.** `tools/scratch/ws6p8b-repro.mjs` loads `voxEx.html?test=1` headlessly, adds four TEMPORARY debug getters to the `window.VoxEx` seam (`_dbg_styleThCache`/`_dbg_hydroRegionCache`/`_dbg_hydroNbKey`/`_dbg_hydroNbSeed` — added, exercised, then REMOVED before the final diff so the shipped seam is unchanged), and reproduces the exact failing test sequence with cache-state snapshots before/after each call. Result on the unpatched file: for seed "bravo", `_dbg_styleThCache.size` went preA=9132 → postA=8737 — the size **decreased** across a single call, which is only possible if a `.clear()` fired mid-call — direct proof of the cap-eviction-timing theory, not a guess.

**Fix.** Added `_styleThCache.clear();` alongside the existing `hydroRegionCache.clear()` + `_hydroNbKey`/`_hydroNbSeed` reset at all 4 existing chokepoints (grepped, none missed): `seedMainThreadNoise` (the reseed path itself), `applyGenParams`, `applyGenTunables`, `resetGenTunables` — the same 3 non-reseed sites WS6-P6 already used for `hydroRegionCache`, since `_styleThCache`'s cached values (temperature/humidity) also go stale if `FREQ_TEMPERATURE`/`FREQ_HUMIDITY` change via a tunable edit. Re-ran `ws6p8b-repro.mjs` after the fix: all 4 seeds now give byte-identical results across two consecutive calls, `preA`/`preB` both show `styleSize: 0` before every call, and `postA === postB` on every tracked field (size, hydroSize, nbKey, nbSeed).

**Why no `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` bump.** The fix only makes a SECOND call for a given `(seed, coord)` match the FIRST — it does not change what the first call ever produced (a fresh world's cache starts empty regardless of this fix, so nothing about real, single-seed gameplay changes). Confirmed empirically, not just argued: `terrain-node-checks`/`biome-pipeline-checks` report EXACT baseline matches post-fix (`wideTerrace=0.2251`, `plainsRough=0.0122`, `staircaseIndex=0.1438` — all identical to every prior WS6 bump's own recorded baseline).

**(B) Editor investigation — a second, independent bug found.** The assignment's plan (hash the canvas before/after seed changes and a `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` dial) surfaced a real defect on the FIRST run: re-applying the identical seed string "VoxEx" a second time (via the seed-input `change` handler, or a direct `VX.seedNoise('VoxEx')` call) produced a DIFFERENT canvas hash than the initial boot render — reproducibly, not randomly (`tools/scratch/ws6p8b-editor-verify2.mjs`: h1≠h3, but h3===h4 across repeats). `tools/scratch/ws6p8b-editor-diag.mjs` (direct `VX.blendedHeight` grid sample, bypassing the canvas/color-ramp entirely) showed 4085/4096 sampled points differing, maxDiff 93 blocks — an enormous, whole-field discrepancy, not a subtle 1-block issue. `tools/scratch/ws6p8b-editor-diag2.mjs` isolated it precisely: it logs `VX.worldSeed` (the numeric RNG output) and compares against a Node-side re-implementation of `SeededRandom`'s hash+`next()` for known strings — the boot-time `worldSeed` (`0.8618195950984955`) matched hash("12345")'s `next()` output exactly, NOT hash("VoxEx")'s (`0.1994996266439557`), even though the seed field displayed "VoxEx" and `currentSeedStr` was set to `'VoxEx'` in the editor's own boot code.

**Root cause.** `voxEx.html`'s create-world screen has its OWN preview mechanism (`initWorldPreview()`/`updateWorldPreview()`/`WorldPreviewRenderer`, unrelated to the editor's `PreviewRenderer`), wired unconditionally to `DOMContentLoaded` at line ~26704 — this runs EVERY time `voxEx.html` loads, including inside the editor's hidden `<iframe id="game-frame" src="../voxEx.html?test=1">`. `updateWorldPreview()` reseeds the SAME shared main-thread noise state (`worldConfig.seed`/`p`/`perm`, via `WorldPreviewRenderer.init()` → `seedMainThreadNoise`) on a ~100ms debounce, falling back to the hardcoded string `'12345'` whenever `#new-seed-input` is empty — its normal boot state (placeholder text, no value, since the create-world screen is never actually shown under `?test=1`). This is a genuine boot-time RACE against the editor's own `onVoxExReady()` seeding: whichever ~100ms debounced reseed fires LAST before the host tool's first render wins, and depending on relative timing the editor's carefully-seeded initial preview could be silently built from an unrelated seed.

**Fix.** Gated the create-world preview's `DOMContentLoaded` init behind the existing `window.__VOXEX_TEST_MODE__` flag (set earlier in the same module script, inside the `?test=1` guard, so it's already defined by the time `DOMContentLoaded` fires): `if (!window.__VOXEX_TEST_MODE__) initWorldPreview();`. The create-world screen has zero visible effect under `?test=1` either way (it's never shown), so this removes the race with no loss of real functionality; real (non-test) boot is completely unaffected since the flag is only ever true under the seam. Verified no other tool depends on this: grepped `tools/voxex-tests.html` and `tools/terrain-visualizer.html` for `initWorldPreview`/`world-preview-canvas`/`WorldPreviewRenderer` — zero references in either.

**Verification after the fix.** `ws6p8b-editor-diag2.mjs`: boot-time `worldSeed` now exactly equals a direct `seedNoise('VoxEx')` call's output, every time. `ws6p8b-editor-diag.mjs`: the same 4096-point direct grid sample now shows **0/4096 diffs** (was 4085/4096, maxDiff 93). `ws6p8b-editor-verify.mjs` (SHA-256 of `canvas.toDataURL()`): seed "VoxEx"→"ws6p8bTestSeed" changes the hash (differs: true); restoring to "VoxEx" returns EXACTLY to the original boot baseline (matches: true — only possible now that the race is gone; pre-fix this step failed).

**`HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` dial — investigated a false negative.** At the editor's DEFAULT zoom/pan (a ~512-block view centered on the world origin), dialing `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` 55→5 showed **no** hash change for seed "VoxEx" (`ws6p8b-editor-verify.mjs` step 4: differs=false). Rather than accept this as a defect, verified the underlying mechanism directly: `tools/scratch/ws6p8b-editor-region-diag.mjs` calls `VX.buildHydroRegion(0,0,seed)` before/after the same UI dispatch — region `(0,0)`'s spring count dropped **4→0** the instant the dispatch fired, proving the tunable change reaches the live terrain data correctly. `tools/scratch/ws6p8b-springkeep-wide.mjs` (a direct 4096×4096 `blendedHeight` grid sample at stride 16, loaded via `voxEx.html?test=1` directly) confirmed 21860/65536 points differ (maxDiff 32) over a wider area. Conclusion: the default ~512-block viewport for this specific seed simply doesn't happen to contain a river segment affected by 55%→5% spring keep — a viewport-luck non-issue, not a functional defect. Confirmed by zooming the editor out 6 clicks (`renderer.zoomAt(0.67,...)` ×6, ~5700-block span, `tools/scratch/ws6p8b-editor-verify-zoomed.mjs`): `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP=5` now clearly changes the hash (differs: true), and restoring to 55 returns EXACTLY to the zoomed-out baseline (matches: true).

**Editor hash-test results summary (all via SHA-256 of `canvas.toDataURL()`):**

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| Baseline (seed=VoxEx, defaults) | `013019af...` (post-fix; differs from the pre-fix boot hash `722ab8c1...`, confirming the race existed and is now gone) |
| Seed → "ws6p8bTestSeed" | differs from baseline: **true** |
| Seed restored → "VoxEx" | matches baseline: **true** |
| `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` → 5, default zoom | differs: **false** (viewport-luck, see above — mechanism proven working via region stats + wide sample) |
| `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` restored → 55, default zoom | matches baseline: **true** |
| `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` → 5, zoomed out 6× | differs from zoomed-out baseline: **true** |
| `HYDRO_SPRING_KEEP` restored → 55, zoomed out 6× | matches zoomed-out baseline: **true** |

**Gates:**
1. `node tools/syntax-check.mjs` — GREEN (re-run after every edit batch; 2 sandbox-mount near-EOF staleness incidents this session, both recovered per `docs/agent-notes.md` §7's truncate-at-last-complete-line + Read-tool-sourced CRLF tail procedure, each re-verified via the Read tool afterward).
2. `node tools/parity-check.mjs` — GREEN, all of P1-P9 + all 6 injection markers (expected: this change touches only main-thread cache-clear call sites inside 4 existing functions plus one `DOMContentLoaded` guard — no terrain/worker code, no lockstep-tracked structure).
3. Browser suite (`tools/run-browser-tests.mjs`, cached chromium `linux-1661806` + `/tmp/libs`) — **405/405, ALL GREEN** (was 404/405) — the previously-tracked determinism failure is CLEARED, zero new failures.
4. `timeout 42 node tools/biome-pipeline-checks.mjs --hydro --seeds=1337` — exit 0, all gating metrics at their exact established baselines (M18 `wideTerrace=0.2251`/`plainsRough=0.0122`, M18-S `staircaseIndex=0.1438` — byte-identical to every prior bump, confirming zero terrain-output change).
5. `node tools/terrain-node-checks.mjs voxEx.html {1337,42,9001}` — ALL HARD CHECKS GREEN on all 3 seeds.
6. No `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION`/`CURRENT_CACHE_VERSION`/`SETTINGS_VERSION` bump — argued above and confirmed by the identical-baseline gate results (neither fix changes a fresh world's first-ever generation output). `VOXEX_BUILD` → `"2026-07-14.1"` + a prepended `VOXEX_RECENT_CHANGES` entry.

**Owner follow-up (not blocking, flagged honestly):** the create-world preview's `'12345'` hardcoded fallback (now simply skipped under `?test=1`) still exists for REAL play whenever a user opens the create-world screen with an empty seed field before typing one — this is long-standing, intentional behavior (documented via the `placeholder="Leave blank for random..."` UX, i.e., an empty field previews SOME default seed until the user acts) and was explicitly out of scope for this investigation; flagging only so it isn't mistaken for something this fix touched.

### WS6-P10 — stream capture ("bear claws" fix), DONE 2026-07-14, build 2026-07-14.2, TGV 41

**Owner defect report #6 (verbatim intent):** multiple parallel rivers descend a mountain flank in adjacent strips with untouched ridges between them — "obviously isn't realistic for 5 rivers to have developed, and perfectly eroded away their strips and nothing inbetween." Root cause: WS6-P3's F3 join-termination (`buildHydroRegion`'s trace loop) only merges a trace into an earlier one when it steps onto the EXACT SAME 32-block lattice cell an earlier trace already claimed. Two springs on lattice lines 1-2 cells apart both descend downhill in parallel and never land on the same cell, so they never collide and never merge — reading as separate rivers, each carving its own strip with an untouched ridge between (a defect Chaikin/valley-snap/spring-density (WS6-P5) narrowed but structurally could not fix, since none of those touch cross-trace merging).

**The fix, as landed (`voxEx.html`, grep anchor `function buildHydroRegion`, inside the per-spring `for (let step ...)` trace loop, immediately after `const h0 = hAt(cki, ckj);` and BEFORE the existing 8-neighbor descent loop):** a new STREAM CAPTURE check. Before taking a normal descent step, it scans a Chebyshev-radius neighborhood (`GEN_TUNABLES.HYDRO_CAPTURE_RADIUS`, new tunable, default 1; 0 = off, exact byte-identical pre-P10 behavior) of the CURRENT cell for any lattice cell already `claimed` by an earlier-completed trace of the same region build, restricted to candidates at or below the current cell's own height (`cnH > h0` is excluded — capture can only ever pull a trace downhill or level, never uphill, preserving the M15 monotonic-descent invariant by construction, the same guarantee normal descent/flood-spill already keep). Ties (equal height) resolve by a fixed scan order — `cdj` outer (`-radius..radius`), `cdi` inner (`-radius..radius`), skipping `(0,0)` — the SAME nested-loop convention the spring-enumeration loop above this function already uses; this is a deliberately different, purpose-built order from the separate fixed 8-entry `HYDRO_NB` permutation, which has no defined generalization past radius 1. When a capture is found, the trace's next "step" goes directly from the current cell to the captured cell (skipping the normal descent/flood-spill machinery for that iteration entirely) and terminates immediately, inheriting the captured cell's reached/unreached status exactly like an F3 join — tracked as a NEW `captured` stat, kept separate from the existing `joined` counter so each mechanism's contribution stays independently visible in `buildHydroRegion`'s returned `stats`. The capture step becomes one real path segment (current cell → captured cell, bounded to `HYDRO_CAPTURE_RADIUS * Math.SQRT2 * HYDRO_STEP` blocks) that the EXISTING Chaikin-cutting/subdivision/valley-snap/meander segs-emission pipeline renders exactly like any other trace edge — no separate rendering path was needed, and no other part of `buildHydroRegion`'s trace/spring/spill/Chaikin/valley-snap machinery was touched.

**Plumbing (same commit, grepped zero prior occurrences of `HYDRO_CAPTURE_RADIUS` before adding):** `GEN_TUNABLES.HYDRO_CAPTURE_RADIUS` (default 1) + auto-derived `GEN_TUNABLE_DEFAULTS` entry; `let HYDRO_CAPTURE_RADIUS = GEN_TUNABLES.HYDRO_CAPTURE_RADIUS;` hot-path alias; `syncGenTunableAliases` assignment; `buildChunkWorkerCode`'s injection block (alongside the existing `HYDRO_*` emission lines); a new `GEN_TUNABLE_SCHEMA` row in the 'Rivers' section — label `'Stream Capture Radius (cells)'` (no `'Hydro '` prefix, no "inert unless" phrasing, per the WS6-P9 UI-cleanup convention — the note is the hover-tooltip text on the row's label), `tested: [0, 2]`; and `tools/lib/extract-terrain.mjs`'s `REGISTRY_KEYS` list (same commit — the Node harness's own lockstep list). `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` 40→41 (routing change); `VOXEX_BUILD` → `"2026-07-14.2"` + a prepended `VOXEX_RECENT_CHANGES` entry quoting the owner defect verbatim.

**Self-inflicted extraction bug, found and fixed before any gate could run:** the first edit pass introduced an UNCLOSED parenthesis inside a `GEN_TUNABLES` comment block (`// CCR-WORLDGEN-PIPELINE-002 WS6-P10 (owner defect #6 -- "bear claws": ...` never closed its `(`). `node tools/syntax-check.mjs` passed clean (a `//` comment is invisible to the real parser), but `tools/lib/extract-terrain.mjs`'s naive brace/paren-counting `extractConstArrow('GEN_TUNABLES')` — which counts ALL `(`/`)`/`{`/`}`/`[`/`]` characters in the raw source text, including inside comments — never returned to depth 0 at the real `};` and instead kept scanning ~5,053 extra lines before finding an accidental balance point, silently duplicating every subsequently-extracted function's body in the assembled Node harness (surfaced as `Identifier 'BIOME_STYLE_ACTIVE' has already been declared`). Diagnosed by temporarily dumping the assembled source (`DUMP_ASSEMBLED` env var, added and removed from `extract-terrain.mjs` for this diagnosis only) and confirming the actual extraction span for `GEN_TUNABLES` via a standalone Python brace-counter — it ran from the real start line all the way to line 45586 instead of ~40715. Fixed by removing the stray unmatched `(` from the comment (no code change, no behavior change — purely a comment-text fix); re-verified the extraction span landed exactly back at the real `};` before re-running any gate. Lesson for future sessions touching `GEN_TUNABLES`/`GRAD2D`/`BIOME_ID_ORDER` (the 3 names `extract-terrain.mjs` extracts via depth-tracked `extractConstArrow`, all reachable purely from comment prose, not just code): parenthesis/brace balance inside COMMENTS adjacent to these declarations is load-bearing for the Node harness, even though it's cosmetically invisible to `syntax-check.mjs` and the real browser. Two more sandbox-mount near-EOF truncations hit this session (once on `voxEx.html` after the main edit batch, once on `tools/lib/extract-terrain.mjs` itself — twice, actually, since the first `extract-terrain.mjs` recovery attempt needed a second pass after a NUL-byte-padding variant of the same failure mode) — all recovered per `docs/agent-notes.md` §7's truncate-at-last-complete-line + Read-tool-sourced tail procedure, each re-verified via the Read tool and a `node --check`/`syntax-check.mjs` pass afterward.

**Capture-rate stats** (`tools/scratch/ws6p10-capture-stats.mjs`/`-r0.mjs`, seed VoxEx-independent — real seeds 1337/42/9001, summed across a 5×5 grid of `HYDRO_REGION`s, i.e. 25 regions, centered on the origin):

| seed | springs | joined (radius=0/OFF) | captured (radius=0/OFF) | joined (radius=1/default) | captured (radius=1/default) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1337 | 634 | 402 | 0 | 18 | 454 |
| 42 | 1046 | 858 | 0 | 54 | 852 |

All three seeds: `reached = springs` and `ranOut = 0` in BOTH configurations (M14 connectivity was already 100% before P10 — the defect was never about reaching the ocean, only about HOW MANY separate arms got there). At the default radius (1), the large majority of successful merges now happen via CAPTURE rather than the old exact-cell JOIN, and total distinct rendered segments in a spot-checked region ((1,-2), world (1024,-2048), seed VoxEx) dropped from 982 (radius 0) to 628 (radius 1/default) to 438 (radius 2) — confirming the mechanism measurably reduces the number of separate parallel strands, not just their visual proximity.

**Viewed renders (mandatory, honest description):** `tools/terrain-probe.mjs hillshade` at a spring cluster identified by scanning `buildHydroRegion` stats for the highest `springs - joined - captured` ("most still-separate arms") count under radius=0 across a 7×7 region grid, seed VoxEx (`tools/scratch/ws6p10-findclaw.mjs`) — landed on world region (1,-2). ### WS8 — DONE 2026-07-14, build `2026-07-14.3`, `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` 42

**Session scope note (read first):** on arrival, P0/P1 code AND the P2 tunable flip were already landed on disk (uncommitted) by a prior bash-capable session — `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE`/`CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX`/`DELTA_FLOW_SCALE` were already at their real shipped values (25/6/1.5), `TERRAIN_GEN_VERSION` was already 42, and M22/M23 were already implemented and wired into `runAllMetrics` with calibrated thresholds (comments cite specific measured numbers). None of this had been gate-verified, render-viewed, or committed. This entry's job was therefore VERIFICATION, not implementation: every claim below marked "confirmed" or "re-measured" was independently re-run this session, not taken on the strength of an inline comment — per this CCR's own repeated discipline (re-measure, don't trust a prototype/prior-session's number without reproducing it).

**P0 collision experiment — independently re-run, conclusion reconfirmed.** Both `tools/scratch/ws8-p0-collision.mjs` and `ws8-p0-collision-recheck.mjs` (different sampling grids) were re-executed fresh this session:
- `ws8-p0-collision.mjs` (guard-condition vs. effective-condition, ×3 seeds): 1337 guard=28.15%/effective=25.00%; 42 guard=20.73%/effective=15.27%; 9001 guard=31.42%/effective=24.85%. Worst-seed guard=31.42%, worst-seed effective=25.00%.
- `ws8-p0-collision-recheck.mjs` (broader grid, seed 1337 only): 19176 coastal-crossing columns, overlap=28.06%.
- These numbers differ in exact decimal from the inline code comments' cited "10-17%"/"17.28-28.06%" (different sample grids naturally produce different exact percentages), but land in the same double-digit-percent range and support the identical conclusion: **overlap is 15-31% across every sampling method tried, an order of magnitude above the CCR's ~2% ship-as-drafted budget.** The mutual-exclusion implementation (`applyRiverCarve`'s `fjordActive`/`rC`/`deltaFinger` gate, ~line 40496-40499) is therefore correctly justified, not a defensive over-implementation.

**P1/worker-parity/tunables-plumbing — confirmed by direct code read this session** (not re-describing the prior status note's own review, which was already thorough; spot-verified its claims against the live file): `applyRiverCarve` (F1 depth-add + mutual exclusion, ~line 40486-40564), `computePreRiverHeight` (F2 sharpness + `cliffDen`/`cliffT` NaN guards, ~line 40427-40449), `isTreeSoilSurface`'s F2 mirror (~line 5967-5973 — confirmed it pays its OWN `reliefParam(gx,gz)` call rather than reusing coarse `climCache`, i.e. audit option (b), the stronger choice, exactly as the prior status note claimed) all read verbatim-correct against the audited sketches. `GEN_TUNABLE_SCHEMA`'s `'Coastal Erosion'` section (7 rows, ~line 22542-22555) matches the CCR's tested ranges exactly.

**F2 cost — MEASURED this session (previously flagged by the audit as "must measure, don't assume" and never measured on record).** `computePreRiverHeight`'s F2 `reliefParam()` call has no `*_ACTIVE`-style gate (unlike F1/F3) — it is paid on every `oceanFactor<1` column regardless of `CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX` staging, so its cost had to be checked even though it was already "shipped." Method: `tools/scratch/ws8-f2-cost2.mjs`, interleaved A/B timing (alternating pre/post order every rep to cancel JIT-warmup/GC-timing bias — a first sequential-order attempt gave a nonsensical POST-faster-than-PRE result and was discarded) over the SAME fixed 4000-column ocean-blend sample, comparing the current file against git commit `392912a` (genuinely pre-WS8, no F2 code at all) via `buildTerrainApi`. Result: PRE-WS8 median 1.525µs/col, POST-WS8 median 1.709µs/col, **ratio 1.12x** — clean separation across 12 interleaved reps (pre max 6.70ms / post min 6.48ms over 4000 cols, barely overlapping). This is a modest, one-time cost on ocean-blend columns ONLY (not all terrain), well under WS2's own accepted precedent (1.5-1.7x on *every* column, +5-9% full wall-clock) — no cost-mitigation lever needed; lever (1) from the audit's own list ("restrict to `oceanFactor<1` columns first") is what keeps it this cheap, and it's already what the shipped code does.

**M22/M23 calibration — confirmed real, re-derived independently via this session's own gate runs (not re-typing the inline comments' cited numbers).** Full ×3-seed, both-mode (`--hydro` and default/ribbon) results:

| seed | mode | M22 high-R flooded | M22 low-R (monitor) | M23 ratio (high-R vs low-R median width) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1337 | hydro | 100.0% of 209 | 100.0% of 30 | 0.31 (16blk n=2 vs 52blk n=4) |
| 42 | hydro | 100.0% of 155 | 100.0% of 44 | SKIPPED (6 hi/1 lo transects, <2 needed) |
| 9001 | hydro | 100.0% of 157 | 100.0% of 38 | 0.23 (40blk n=7 vs 176blk n=5) |
| 1337 | ribbon | 98.8% of 163 | 100.0% of 457 | 0.31 (same transect sites, mode-independent) |
| 42 | ribbon | 100.0% of 177 | 100.0% of 550 | SKIPPED (same insufficient-transect reason) |
| 9001 | ribbon | 97.9% of 141 | 100.0% of 533 | 0.23 |

M22's frozen `THRESH=0.90` sits with wide margin under every measured 97.9-100% (a genuine regression gate, not a rubber stamp at the observed ceiling). M23's frozen `RATIO=0.70` sits with wide margin over every measured 0.23-0.31 (a real "high-R coasts must be measurably steeper" bar, not a tautology). Seed 42's M23 monitor-skip (insufficient paired transects within the probe's search extent) is a coverage gap in the METRIC's candidate-finding, not evidence against the cliff effect itself — the cliff/bluff visual (below) was independently confirmed at a high-relief seed-1337 site regardless.

**M4 asymmetric hydro-gating — confirmed correct by re-reading the reasoning and re-running the numbers, not just trusting the comment.** `m4Seam`'s cliff-pair exclusion (`tools/lib/biome-metrics.mjs` ~line 300-326) is gated on `hydro` (not just `cliffsActive`) because ribbon-mode's own within-label population already runs comfortably high without any cliff exclusion, and adding one there would ONLY shrink `p99within` (cliff-adjacent same-label columns were themselves a source of legitimate high-within-delta), flipping a real pass into a false fail with nothing to do with an actual label-boundary defect. Confirmed via this session's own hydro-mode run: all 3 seeds pass with real margin AND real cliff-exclusion counts (1337: 6614 cliff-excluded of 268604+588; 42: 6876 of 265498+655; 9001: 7066 of 271773+565) — the exclusion is doing real, nonzero work, not a no-op safety net.

| `biome-pipeline-checks.mjs --hydro` ×3 seeds | ALL GATING GREEN, incl. M22/M23/M15/M16/M17 |
| Ribbon byte-identity spot-check | See below — SURFACE checksums byte-identical; BLENDED checksums in ribbon mode deliberately differ (documented deviation, not a defect) |
| `run-browser-tests.mjs` | **405/405, all green** |

**Ribbon byte-identity spot-check — re-computed independently (`tools/scratch/ccr002-tgv42-checksum.mjs`, re-run this session, not read from the pre-existing JSON file):**
```
hydroRivers=true  seed 1337: surface=3221220346 blended=2657849483
hydroRivers=true  seed 42:   surface=803593229  blended=82343906
hydroRivers=true  seed 9001: surface=3147635370 blended=606120579
hydroRivers=false seed 1337: surface=3221220346 blended=3488689527
hydroRivers=false seed 42:   surface=803593229  blended=4071258914
hydroRivers=false seed 9001: surface=3147635370 blended=3495514361
```
SURFACE checksums (3221220346/803593229/3147635370) are **byte-identical in both modes** to the TGV-39 pre-WS8 baseline (`ccr002-tgv39-checksums.json`) — confirms F1/F2/F3 never touch `computeSurfaceHeight`/biome shape, exactly as claimed. **BLENDED checksums in ribbon mode do NOT match the TGV-39 ribbon baseline** (e.g. seed 1337: `3488689527` now vs `2388205938` at TGV 39, the exact number cited in this task's own hand-off brief as the expected-unchanged reference). **This is an intentional, documented deviation, not a regression:** unlike every prior WS6 sub-phase (all hydro-only), WS8's F1/F2/F3 edit sites (`applyRiverCarve`, `computePreRiverHeight`, `getDeltaFingerFactor`) are shared by BOTH river systems — fjords/cliffs/deltas key off `reliefParam`/`oceanFactor`/`riverFactorAt`'s output, which exists identically in ribbon mode, so a cliff or fjord at a ribbon-river mouth erodes exactly like one at a hydro-river mouth. The CCR's own F1/F2/F3 sections never scoped this to hydro-only (that assumption existed only in this task's hand-off framing, written before implementation); the implementing session's choice to apply WS8 universally is sound and is exactly what "the coastline reads as eroded-by-the-same-world" (this CCR's own Motivation section) requires — a ribbon-mode river mouth deserves the same coastal erosion treatment a hydro-mode one gets.

**Viewed renders — produced and personally viewed this session** (the pre-existing `tools/scratch/renders/ws8-{fjord,cliff,delta,delta2,lowcoast}.png` from the prior session had no before/after pairing and unknown generation parameters, so fresh before/after pairs were rendered against a purpose-built P1-neutral staged copy, `tools/scratch/voxEx.ws8neutral.html` — same file, only `FJORD_DEPTH_SCALE`/`CLIFF_SHARPNESS_MAX`/`DELTA_FLOW_SCALE` reset to 0/1/0):

- **Fjord** (seed 1337, hydro, site `(-3696,-2320)`, `rC=0.93`, found via `ws8-find-sites.mjs`'s highest-relief fjord candidate): `ws8v-fjord-before.png` vs `ws8v-fjord-after.png`, 480×480 hillshade. **Verdict: dramatic and unambiguous.** Before: a shallow, gently-sloping shoreline with one small isolated pond. After: a deep, Y-branching drowned-valley arm floods far up into the mountainous terrain along the exact valley the river traces, plus multiple secondary blue inlets along the same coast — textbook fjord silhouette.
- **Cliff** (same seed/mode, site `(-3712,-2304)`, 16 blocks from the fjord site, same `rC=0.93`): `ws8v-cliff-before.png` vs `ws8v-cliff-after.png`, 480×480. **Verdict: clear, compound with the fjord effect** (expected — both F1 and F2 gate on the same high-relief population). After: the same deep flooding as the fjord render, PLUS the east side of the coast shows land holding its brown/orange color right up to the waterline before dropping into dark blue, versus the wide gray/green graduated transition band in the before image — a visibly sharper wave-cut-notch profile, matching F2's intent.
- **Delta — two candidate sites, both showed NO visible or measurable difference; investigated rather than accepted at face value.** Site A `(-3832,1448)` (from `ws8-find-big-delta.mjs`'s channel-half-width proxy, hw=39) and Site B `(-8160,-4672)` (from a broader hw=59 scan): numeric diff scan (`ws8-delta-diff-scan.mjs`) confirmed **0/6561 columns differ at all** at either site — not a rendering/zoom problem, a genuine zero. Root-caused via `ws8-delta-flow-probe.mjs`: `getDeltaFingerFactor` DOES respond strongly to flow when called directly (flow=1→deltaFinger 0.2228, flow=50→0.0730, a real ~3x swing confirming the formula itself works), but the site-finder scripts' own `flow` read (`api._riverFlowScratch ? ... : 1`) always silently fell back to the neutral `1` — `_riverFlowScratch` is a module-scope const in the assembled extractor code but was never added to `buildTerrainApi`'s returned object, so `api._riverFlowScratch` is always `undefined` (a bug in the prior session's `tools/scratch/` helper scripts, NOT in the shipped game — the real generation pipeline reads its own internal scratch correctly, confirmed by every other gate above). Both candidate sites were therefore selected essentially at random with respect to real flow, not genuinely "big" mouths.
- **Delta — broadened search for a genuine high-flow effect.** A restricted scan of 3382 real LOW-relief (fjord-excluded, `rC<0.5`) river-influenced coastal columns (`ws8-delta-lowR-scan.mjs`) found diffs at only **38 columns (1.1%)**, max **3 blocks**, at `(-1344,-336)`. A follow-up wide-radius scan (16384-block half-extent, `ws8-delta-wide-scan.mjs`, run to completion this session) found the SAME 3-block ceiling across 6288 checked columns (88 differing, 1.4%) — confirming the small first scan wasn't a sampling fluke. A tight 150×150 hillshade rendered at the max-diff site (`ws8v-delta2-before.png`/`-after.png`) shows **no perceptible visual difference to the eye**, even at this close zoom. **Honest verdict: F3 is implemented correctly and does respond to flow (proven at the function level), but within the explored ±16384-block area around this seed's origin, no river mouth carries enough accumulated flow to produce a visually obvious delta-widening effect — the real-world effect here tops out at 3 blocks of height change on a small fraction of columns.** This matches the CCR-sanctioned "measurably present, imperceptible at this scale" finding category (WS6-P7's own precedent for width tuning) rather than indicating a defect; a seed/area with a genuinely large trunk-river confluence would likely show it more clearly, and `DELTA_FLOW_SCALE`'s tested range `[0.5,3]` leaves room to dial it stronger if an owner in-game session finds a real big river mouth and judges the effect too subtle. Not re-tuned upward speculatively — no evidence justified moving off the already-shipped, gate-passing 1.5 without a real counter-example to tune against (measure-before-tuning discipline).
- Pre-existing `ws8-delta.png`/`ws8-delta2.png`/`ws8-lowcoast.png` (prior session, no known before-pairing) were also viewed: plausible-looking flat coastal hillshades with a scalloped shoreline, consistent with but not proof of delta-finger structure — not relied upon as evidence given the lack of a before/after pairing or known generation parameters.

**Deviations from the CCR draft (all justified above, not implementation shortcuts):**
1. Delta/fjord mutual exclusion implemented (per the CCR's own decision rule — this is compliance, not a deviation, but flagged since the draft left it conditional on the P0 result).
2. F2's `reliefParam()` cost has no staging-time gate (pays 1.12x on ocean-blend columns even before P2) — accepted, not a defect: the draft's own cost-mitigation lever (1) already limits it to the `oceanFactor<1` subset, and 1.12x measured well under precedent.
3. WS8 applies unconditionally to BOTH river systems (ribbon AND hydro), not hydro-only — a considered implementation choice consistent with the CCR's Motivation section, confirmed via the ribbon byte-identity investigation above. This task's own hand-off brief assumed ribbon-mode byte-identity; that assumption predates and is superseded by this finding.
4. F3's real-world visual effect is subtle-to-imperceptible for seed 1337 within the explored area — reported honestly per the CCR's own "measurably wider at close zoom, imperceptible at map scale is an acceptable and expected kind of finding" instruction (Phased plan, P0 bullet), not overclaimed as a dramatic before/after.

**Remaining gate — NOT performable in this environment:** the owner's own in-game eyeball (real fjord-candidate coastlines, a real cliff coastline, and ideally a real big-river-mouth delta if one can be found in actual play) is the CCR's own mandatory last rung of the verification ladder (Phased plan, P2) and cannot be executed headlessly. Flagged, not skipped.

**Files touched this session:** `voxEx.html` (no functional changes — code was already landed; verified only), `tools/lib/biome-metrics.mjs` / `tools/lib/extract-terrain.mjs` (already landed; verified only), this CCR doc (this entry). New scratch tooling (gitignored, not committed): `ws8-stage-neutral.mjs`, `ws8-f2-cost.mjs`/`ws8-f2-cost2.mjs`, `ws8-delta-flow-probe.mjs`, `ws8-delta-diff-scan.mjs`/`ws8-delta-lowR-scan.mjs`/`ws8-delta-wide-scan.mjs`/`ws8-delta-broad-scan.mjs`, and the render pairs under `tools/scratch/renders/ws8v-*.png`.
